Mini ticket (illustrative) — Own 100 sh @ 100. Collar: buy 95 put + sell 108 call, 90 DTE, IV 30%. Put debit ≈ $2.10, call credit ≈ $2.00 → net cost ≈ $0.10/sh ($10). Floor $95 (max loss ≈ $510 incl. net), ceiling $108 (max gain ≈ $790). Near-zero-cost protection in exchange for a capped top. (Full worked TICKET lives in L1-3 CSP.)

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen on Barchart / OptionStrat (free): the concentrated long you must hedge, put & call OI > 500 at target strikes, bid/ask < 8% of mid, prefer names with LIQUID LEAPS for cheaper per-day cover, check skew (steep skew = costly puts → favor collar). Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): apply to any large concentrated holding — e.g. hedging a NVDA or AAPL RSU stack via a 90-day collar (deep, liquid chains).

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha is over-insuring: paying premium drag year after year for crashes that rarely come, so the cumulative insurance cost quietly exceeds the losses avoided — the protective put's "tax." For the collar, the gotcha is getting capped in the one big rally that would have made the year, plus ex-div early assignment on the short call (F4).

Honest edge — the metric is drawdown avoided vs cumulative hedge cost (premium drag + upside forfeited), over a full cycle. Verdict: Genuine as RISK MANAGEMENT for a position you genuinely cannot exit — NOT as an alpha source. The edge is behavioral (it lets you HOLD through a scare), not statistical; size and IV-regime-time the hedge or the drag eats you.

Trade now

Vertical spreads (credit when IVR is high, debit when low), iron condors on liquid range-bound names, and PMCC.

Theory to internalize

The Greeks (F1) and IV/IVR & the surface (F2) drive every structure here. POP is not EV — the tail can eat the year.

Avoid

Calendars/ratios until you grok vega-sign and short-gamma; selling condors/strangles through earnings.

L2 · Intermediate

Straddles & Strangles 跨式與勒式

Two-leg pure-volatility bets: buy both legs to be long-vol into a move bigger than priced; sell both to harvest premium when realized stays inside the implied move.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Greeks · IV & surface (POP/EV) · assignment & pin

① 機制 Mechanism

A straddle = a call and a put at the same strike, same expiry. A strangle = a call and a put at different OTM strikes (e.g. 16-delta each), same expiry. Direction cancels out — you are trading the size of the move, not its sign. Long (buy both, debit) = long-gamma, long-vega, theta-negative: you win if the underlying travels far OR if implied vol rises. Short (sell both, credit) = short-gamma, short-vega, theta-positive: you win if the underlying stays put and/or IV falls. The honest core: a long straddle's debit IS the market's priced move — your bet is that forward realized beats implied. The one-sigma expected move a chain prices in is M = S × IV × √(DTE/365). The ATM straddle premium is only ~0.8 of that move (V ≈ M × √(2/π) ≈ 0.80×M), so to recover the 1σ move from the straddle you go the OTHER way — multiply the straddle by ~1.25 (i.e. straddle ÷0.8). The straddle is the cheaper number; the 1σ move is the larger one. A strangle is the same trade with a cheaper debit, a flat dead-zone between strikes, and a wider breakeven gap.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Long straddle shown (debit). Breakevens = strike ± total debit paid — these are set by the premium you actually pay, a SEPARATE quantity from the 1σ expected move (= S × IV × √(DTE/365) ≈ straddle × 1.25). At S=100, IV=0.30, 30 DTE the 1σ move is ≈ ±8.6 (100 × 0.30 × √(30/365)), so the ATM straddle costs ≈ $6.9 (0.8 × 8.6) and breakevens sit at ≈ 93.1 / 106.9 — i.e. just inside the ±1σ band, which is why a long straddle is roughly a coin-flip to touch a breakeven. Long max loss = the debit (at the strike at expiry); long max profit = unbounded up, large down. The short straddle/strangle is the mirror: capped credit, theoretically unbounded loss — that is the trade you must size by max-loss assumptions, not by the credit.

Unbounded ↑ / large ↓long max profit
= net debitlong max loss (at K)
K ± debit · BE inside ±1σ · POP ≈ 40–45%breakevens / POP (long)

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Long straddle: Δ ≈ 0 at the strike (it flips from −1 well below K to +1 well above K as spot crosses K — that's the long gamma), Γ large positive and peaked at K, V large positive, Θ large negative and worst near expiry. The whole P&L is a race: gamma's realized-move gains vs theta's daily bleed. Strangle: same signs but lower magnitude per dollar — gamma/vega are spread across two OTM strikes, so it decays slower and needs a bigger move. Short versions flip every sign: short gamma is the real risk (a gap re-prices your delta against you before you can react).

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

Long straddle/strangle is built for ONE cell: a big move expected, IV still cheap relative to the catalyst — i.e. IV/IVR is low or the priced expected move is smaller than your forecast realized move. It fails when you buy it into an event with rich IV: the post-event vol crush can erase your debit even if the stock moves, because vega losses swamp gamma gains. Short straddle/strangle is the inverse cell: high IVR, no catalyst inside DTE, expectation that realized < implied (harvesting the variance risk premium — compensation for being short gamma into realized-vol risk, with vega a separate, often-hedgeable exposure). The deadly mismatch for shorts: high IVR caused by a real pending shock — you collect a fat credit right before the move that defines your year's worst loss.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

NVDA Q1-FY25 earnings, reported 22 May 2024 (as-of 2026-06-17). Going in, the options chain priced a one-sigma expected move of roughly ±8% (est.) — recovered as ATM-straddle ×1.25 (the straddle premium itself was only ≈ ±6.4% of spot; pre-split prices). The stock then gapped about +9.3% the next session (+9.32% close-to-close, verified TipRanks / optionslam). So the long straddle holder was right on direction-agnostic magnitude — realized beat the 1σ implied by ~1.3pts and cleared the ~±6.4% breakevens. But the catch: NVDA's IV got crushed ~33 vol-points post-print (est., Market Chameleon). On a +9.3% up-move the call leg is the winner (it gains intrinsic), while the put leg goes to zero on direction; the trouble is that vol crush hit BOTH legs' vega at once, so the call's intrinsic gain was largely offset by the collapse in extrinsic value — a long straddle bought at peak pre-earnings IV barely cleared breakeven despite the 9% move. Lesson: against a known binary, "will it move?" is the easy question — "will it move more than the already-rich priced move, net of crush?" is the trade. Short premium would have profited here only if positioned far enough OTM (a wide strangle) to survive the gap.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
Entry IVR gateLong: IVR < 30 · Short: IVR > 50
Delta / strikeStraddle: ATM · Strangle: 16Δ each side
DTE windowLong event: 7–21 · Short premium: 30–45
Profit targetLong: +50–100% of debit · Short: 25–50% of credit
Stop / time-stopLong: −50% debit or exit pre-crush · Short: 2× credit loss, hard-exit 21 DTE
SizingLong: debit ≤ 1% account (it can zero). Short: UNDEFINED risk — size by assumed 2σ gap max-loss ≤ 3–5% account, never by credit.
Defend triggerActionResulting structureNew risk
Short straddle: spot leaves a strike, one side ITMRoll the untested side toward spot (collect more credit)Inverted / tighter strangleNarrower profit zone; can lock a loss if it whips back
Short strangle tested, IVR still highRoll out in time, keep deltasLater-dated strangle, more creditLonger exposure to the very move you fear
Long straddle: event passed, IV crushed, no moveCut immediately — do not "wait for it"Closed (debit loss)None; preserves capital vs theta bleed-to-zero
Worked trade ticket (short strangle, illustrative / reconstructed est. as-of 2026-06-17) — Underlying SPY @ $540, IVR ~55, no earnings (index). The chain's 1σ expected move over 40 DTE ≈ $540 × 0.16 × √(40/365) ≈ ±$28.6, so 16Δ strikes sit near that ±1σ band. Sell 1× 16Δ put @ $510 and sell 1× 16Δ call @ $565, 40 DTE. Net credit $6.20 ($620/contract). Breakevens = strikes ± credit = $503.80 / $571.20 (wider than the ±1σ band, so realized must beat ~1σ to threaten them). Max profit $620 (both expire worthless); max loss undefined (modeled 2σ down-gap ≈ $3,000+). Reg-T BPR ≈ $8,000–10,000 est. Profit target 50% ($310); stop at 2× credit loss ($1,240); roll the tested side at first strike breach; hard-exit at 21 DTE regardless. Sizing: one contract per ~$80k account so a tail gap is survivable.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Long event-straddle screen: liquid optionable name with a DATED catalyst inside ≤21 DTE; ATM call+put OI >1,000; per-leg bid/ask <5% of mid (slippage kills four-way trades); IVR <30 AND your forecast move > the priced 1σ expected move (= straddle ×1.25, i.e. S × IV × √(DTE/365)). Short premium screen: IVR >50, NO earnings/FDA/macro print inside DTE, index or mega-cap with tight spreads, define-able 2σ max-loss. Free screener: Barchart "Expected Move" / "Long Straddle" screeners + Market Chameleon earnings IV-crush history. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify live): index strangles on SPY/IWM for short-vol; single-name event straddles only on names whose chain shows the priced move undersized vs your view.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: for longs, IV crush — you can be right on the move and still lose because vega bled out (the NVDA lesson). For shorts, short gamma into a gap — the credit is small and certain, the tail loss is large and the thing that eventually arrives; one earnings or macro shock can erase a year of harvested premia.

Honest edge — falsifiable metric: log realized move ÷ priced 1σ expected move (where the priced move = straddle × 1.25 = S × IV × √(DTE/365)) over N≥30 trades. Longs have edge only if your selected names realize >1.0× consistently (rare; the market prices events well). Shorts capture the variance risk premium only if realized stays < implied AND you survive the tail. Verdict: Skill-dependent — the structure is honest, but the priced expected move is an efficient line; edge lives in selection and tail-survival, not in the strategy itself.

L2 · Intermediate

Vertical Spreads 垂直價差

Two-leg, same-expiry, defined-risk directional bets: pay a debit for a cheaper directional shot, or take a credit to sell a probability and let theta work.

Genuine
Prereq: Greeks · IV & POP/EV · liquidity (4 fills) · defined-risk sizing

① 機制 Mechanism

A vertical = long one option, short another of the same type and expiry at a different strike. The short leg caps both your cost/credit AND your payoff — that's the defined-risk box. Four flavors, two families. Debit (long premium, directional): bull-call (buy lower call, sell higher call) and bear-put (buy higher put, sell lower put) — you pay to bet on direction; the short leg subsidizes the long. Credit (short premium, theta): bull-put (sell higher put, buy lower put) and bear-call (sell lower call, buy higher call) — you collect to bet the underlying won't get past your short strike; time and stasis pay you. Key parity fact (cite F3): a bull-call and a bull-put at the same strikes/expiry have identical risk graphs — same max profit, max loss, breakeven. Choose by liquidity, fill quality, and assignment/borrow preference, not by "bullish vs bearish label."

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Bull-put credit spread shown (sell 95 put / buy 90 put). The two key knobs: width (strike distance × 100 = total capital at risk per contract) and credit/debit. For a credit spread: max profit = credit; max loss = width − credit; breakeven = short strike − credit (puts) or + credit (calls). The trade-off is mechanical — collect 1/3 of width and your POP is ~66% with 2:1 loss-to-profit; collect 1/2 of width and POP drops toward 50% at 1:1. There is no free POP; you're paid exactly for the risk you take.

= net creditmax profit
width − creditmax loss
short K − credit · POP ≈ 1−|short Δ|breakeven / POP

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

The defining feature: the short leg truncates every Greek. A credit spread is net theta-positive and modestly short-vega, with delta pointing your direction — but all of them shrink toward zero as both legs approach deep-ITM or deep-OTM. Gamma is double-edged and concentrated near the short strike: that's where small spot moves swing the position fastest and where pin risk lives at expiry (cite F4). A debit spread flips the signs: net theta-negative, net long-vega, but the magnitudes are still capped by the short leg, so it bleeds far slower than a naked long option.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

Credit verticals own the cell: directional-to-neutral lean, HIGH IVR, no catalyst inside DTE — sell elevated premium and let it decay toward your strike staying OTM. They fail when sold in low IVR (thin credit, poor R/R) or when a trend runs through the short strike. Debit verticals own the opposite cell: clear directional view, LOW-to-moderate IVR (you're buying vega, so cheap is better), modest move expected within DTE. They fail on time decay if the move stalls and on vol crush if bought into rich IV. Rule of thumb: sell premium when IV is rich, buy premium when IV is cheap — the IVR gate decides debit-vs-credit more than your conviction does.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

SPX/SPY bull-put credit spreads through the calm 2017 regime (as-of 2026-06-17). In 2017 the S&P rose steadily with VIX averaging in the low-to-mid 11s and realized vol historically low — a textbook environment where systematically selling OTM put spreads on the index harvested premium month after month with high hit-rates, because spot rarely reached short strikes. Then came the abrupt regime change: on 5 Feb 2018 VIX more than doubled, closing at 37.32 from 17.31 (a record +115% day, verified; intraday it printed higher), and short-vol products like XIV imploded ~90% in a session. Defined-risk put-spread sellers lost at most their (small, pre-defined) width-minus-credit — exactly why the spread beats the naked short put or short strangle. Lesson: the credit vertical's whole value proposition is that the long wing converts an open-ended short-vol blowup into a known, survivable, pre-sized loss. The 2017→2018 transition is the cleanest case for why you cap the tail before, not after.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
Entry IVR gateCredit: IVR > 40 · Debit: IVR < 40
Delta / strikeCredit short leg ~16–30Δ · Debit long leg ~50–70Δ
DTE window30–45 (credit) · 30–60 (debit)
Profit targetCredit: 50% of credit · Debit: 50–75% of max value
Stop / time-stopCredit: loss = 2× credit; close/roll at 21 DTE · Debit: −50% debit or thesis broken
SizingDefined-risk: max loss (width − credit) per spread ≤ 1–2% of account; total correlated max-loss ≤ 5%.
Defend triggerActionResulting structureNew risk

Worked trade ticket (bull-put credit spread, reconstructed est. as-of 2026-06-17) — Underlying SPY @ $540, IVR ~45, no earnings. Sell 525 put (~25Δ) / buy 520 put, 40 DTE. Width $5 ($500/contract risk). Net credit $1.55 ($155). Max profit $155; max loss $345; breakeven $523.45; POP ≈ 73%. BPR = max loss = $345. Target +50% ($77); stop at loss = $310 (2× credit); roll-out trigger at 525 breach with >21 DTE; hard-manage at 21 DTE. Sizing: max-loss $345 ≈ 0.4% of an $80k account → up to ~10 contracts within a 4% book cap.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen: underlying with weekly+monthly chains, OI >500 at both target strikes, per-leg bid/ask <5% of mid (four fills compound slippage), IVR gate per family above, NO earnings inside DTE, beta/liquidity high enough that strikes are dense ($1–$5 wide). Prefer the credit family (bull-put / bear-call) when IVR > 40; debit (bull-call / bear-put) when IVR < 40 so you're not buying rich vega. Free screener: Barchart options screener + OptionStrat for visualizing R/R and POP. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify live): index ETFs SPY / QQQ / IWM (tight strikes, no single-name gap risk) for credit; liquid mega-caps for directional debit shots when their IVR is depressed.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: rolling a tested credit spread for a debit — chasing a loser out in time keeps adding risk and converts a small, capped, pre-accepted loss into the trade that hurts. Defined risk only protects you if you actually let it cap. Secondary: pin/assignment on the short leg if spot sits at it into expiry (cite F4) and slippage across four fills on illiquid names.

Honest edge — falsifiable metric: track actual hit-rate vs the entry-implied POP (1 − short delta) and average P&L per trade net of slippage over N≥30. Credit spreads have genuine structural edge when sold in elevated IVR (you're paid the variance risk premium with a hard cap) AND you obey the no-roll-for-debit and 50%-take rules. Verdict: Genuine but modest — the edge is real and durable for disciplined sellers, but it's a small, steady stream that one undisciplined roll can wipe out.

L2 · Intermediate

Iron Condor & Iron Butterfly 鐵兀鷹與鐵蝶

Four-leg, defined-risk range bets: sell two credit spreads around the price to get paid while the underlying stays boxed in and IV deflates.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Vertical spreads · IV & POP/EV · assignment & pin · liquidity (8 fills) · sizing

① 機制 Mechanism

An iron condor (IC) = a bull-put credit spread BELOW the price + a bear-call credit spread ABOVE it, same expiry. You collect two credits and win if spot stays between the two short strikes through expiry. An iron butterfly (IB) is the same construction but with both short strikes at the money (the put-spread and call-spread share the center strike) — it collects a much fatter credit but has a single tiny profit zone instead of a wide one. Both are short-premium, short-gamma, short-vega, theta-positive, market-neutral structures. Think of the IC as renting out a price corridor: you keep the rent (credit) as long as price doesn't leave the building. Compared to a short strangle, the two long wings convert the strangle's open-ended tail into a defined, sized loss (same logic as the vertical's long leg).

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Symmetric IC shown (short 92P/long 87P, short 108C/long 113C). Max profit = total credit, kept anywhere between the short strikes. Max loss = wider-side width − total credit (only ONE side can be breached, so loss is single-side width-based, not both). Breakevens = short put − credit and short call + credit. POP is high (the profit zone is wide) but R/R is poor (you risk multiples of the credit) — the classic income-trade asymmetry. The iron butterfly would show a sharp tent: tiny profit peak at the center, breakevens close in, larger credit, lower POP, better R/R.

= total creditmax profit (zone)
width − creditmax loss (one side)
shortP−cr / shortC+cr · POP ~65–75%breakevens / POP

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

At entry the IC is near delta-neutral (the put-side and call-side deltas offset), net theta-positive (your engine — time passing inside the corridor pays you), net short-vega (an IV spike inflates both short spreads against you), and net short-gamma (the real enemy — as spot drifts toward either short strike, delta accelerates against you and the position becomes increasingly directional). The whole management problem is that short gamma turns a balanced range trade into a one-sided directional loser the moment one side gets tested. The iron butterfly carries far MORE gamma/theta/vega per contract because its shorts are ATM — higher reward, far twitchier to defend.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The IC owns ONE cell precisely: neutral view, HIGH IVR, range-bound/contracting realized vol, NO catalyst inside DTE. You want implied vol elevated at entry (fat credits, room to mean-revert) but forward realized vol low (price stays boxed). It fails in exactly the seductive case — high IVR that's high because a real shock is coming (trend or gap blows through a short strike), or a quiet low-IVR tape where the credits are too thin to justify the tail. The iron butterfly wants the same neutral regime but an even more pinned, dead market — its narrow profit zone needs spot to sit near the center, so it's a tighter, higher-conviction version of the same bet.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Index iron condors across 2017's low-vol regime, stress-tested 5 Feb 2018 (as-of 2026-06-17). Throughout 2017 the S&P ground higher with VIX averaging in the low-to-mid 11s and realized vol near record lows — month after month, SPX/SPY iron condors expired with both sides OTM, the corridor unbreached, full credit kept. But note the discipline problem: 2017 was a persistently LOW-IVR regime, so those entries actually violated this module's own IVR > 50 gate (facet ⑥) — selling cheap, not rich, IV. They only looked good ex post: that's survivorship, the calm before the blow-up, not edge. Worse, that very success bred the trap — complacent sellers widened size and narrowed wings. On 5 Feb 2018 the regime snapped — VIX closed +115% at 37.32 (from 17.31), short-vol ETPs collapsed ~90%. Critically, spot fell and vol spiked simultaneously, so it was the PUT side of standing condors that was the tested/breached side — the short puts got crushed by the down move and the vol expansion at once — while the CALL side stayed untested and expired worthless. Defined-risk condor holders lost their (pre-sized) single-side max — painful but bounded — while undefined short-strangle/XIV sellers were wiped. Lesson: a disciplined condor waits for elevated IVR; selling condors in a low-IVR tape (the 2017 mistake) is picking up pennies in front of the steamroller — thin credits that can't pay for the eventual tested-side max-loss. The iron condor is a positive-carry trade ONLY when entered rich; the long wings + strict sizing are what let you still be in business for the next regime.

Sources: Cboe VIX historical data (5 Feb 2018 close 37.32 vs prior 17.31); SEC / press coverage of the XIV / short-vol ETP collapse, 5–6 Feb 2018.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked trade ticket (SPX iron condor, reconstructed est. as-of 2026-06-17) — Underlying SPX @ 5400, IVR ~55, no macro print inside DTE, 40 DTE. Sell 5250 put / buy 5200 put; sell 5550 call / buy 5600 call (16Δ shorts, 50-wide wings). Total credit $15.00 ($1,500/contract). Max profit $1,500; max loss = $5,000 − $1,500 = $3,500 (one side); breakevens 5235 / 5565; POP ≈ 70%. BPR ≈ $3,500 (defined). Section-1256 tax treatment, cash-settled, no physical-assignment pin (cash-settled) (cite F4). Target +50% ($750); stop at ~2× credit loss; defend tested side per table; hard-exit at 21 DTE. Sizing: $3,500 single-side max ≈ 2% of a $175k account → 1 contract within the book cap.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen: highly liquid, range-prone underlyings (index ETFs / cash-settled index) with deep chains — OI >1,000 at all four short/long strikes, per-leg bid/ask <5% of mid (EIGHT fills, so spread discipline is critical), IVR >50, NO earnings/FDA/FOMC inside DTE, strikes dense enough to place 16Δ shorts and clean wings. Prefer SPX (cash-settled, 1256, no assignment/pin) over single names for the income version. Free screener: Barchart options screener + OptionStrat / Option Alpha condor builders for POP/credit visualization; tastytrade research for management defaults. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify live): SPX (preferred), SPY / QQQ / IWM for smaller accounts — avoid earnings-driven single-name condors unless deliberately trading the vol crush.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: the IC's high POP seduces you into oversizing, and short gamma means a single trend or gap through one short strike hands back many months of credits at once (the 2017→2018 lesson). The defended-side roll buys a little room but can leave you losing on BOTH sides if price whips. On single names, pin/assignment at a tested short strike into expiry is a real, separate risk (cite F4) — which is why cash-settled SPX is the cleaner vehicle.

Honest edge — falsifiable metric: track cumulative credit captured vs cumulative max-loss-days paid out and average P&L per trade net of 8-leg slippage over N≥30, AND confirm you entered at IVR > 50 (selling rich, not cheap, IV). The IC harvests the variance risk premium (compensation for short gamma) with a defined tail, but transaction costs (eight fills) and a few unmanaged tested-side blowups can erase the carry. Verdict: Skill-dependent — genuinely positive expectancy ONLY with high-IVR entry, the 50%/21-DTE discipline, strict sizing, and cash-settled vehicles; without those it decays to crowded-low-edge.

L2 · Intermediate

Broken-Wing & Unbalanced Flies / Condors 偏翼蝶／不對稱兀鷹

A skip-strike butterfly that pushes one wing further out so the structure opens for a NET CREDIT with zero risk on one side — your first taste of buying convexity for free.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Verticals · Iron Condor / Fly · Parity & synthetics · The Greeks

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: a normal butterfly is a long vertical financed by a short vertical sharing the body strike — symmetric wings, debit, dirt-cheap, but a narrow tent. A broken-wing butterfly (BWB) keeps the two short body contracts but moves ONE long wing further away (you "skip" one or more strikes). Mechanically a put BWB = buy 1 ITM/ATM put, sell 2 lower puts (the body), buy 1 further-out put for protection — but with the lower long pushed an extra strike down. Because that far wing is cheaper, the long vertical you keep is worth more than the short vertical you pay away, so the whole package often comes in for a net credit. The trade-off: the far side is now wider than the near side, so the structure carries a real (but defined) loss on the broken-wing side and ZERO loss on the other side. Read it as: a near long vertical (here the 100/95 put spread, 5 wide) subsidized by a WIDER far short vertical (95/87, 8 wide), where the subsidy is large enough to flip the cost to a credit. The net delta lands mildly POSITIVE (the two short body 95P dominate), so the overall structure leans gently bullish — note this comes from the package as a whole, NOT from the upper long put spread (which is itself the bearish/long component). Unbalanced condors apply the same logic to a 4-strike body. This is the cleanest retail bridge to the L3 idea of buying convexity — you are paid to hold a position that profits from a drift back toward your body strike.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

+$545max profit (peak at body 95)
−$255max loss (broken side, below 87)
89.55 / no upside riskdownside breakeven / upside = keep credit

This put BWB (long 100P, short two 95P, long 87P) skips a strike on the lower wing. Entry premiums (per share): pay 3.80 for the 100P, collect 2.20 on each 95P, pay 0.15 for the cheap skipped 87P → net cash 2·2.20 − 3.80 − 0.15 = +0.45 CREDIT (+$45 per spread). Above the highest strike (here 100) all puts expire worthless and you keep that $45 credit — zero upside risk. Peak P&L sits at the body (95): the 100P is worth its 5.00 intrinsic, the other legs are worthless, so payoff = 5.00 + 0.45 = $5.45 → +$545. Below the broken wing (87) the net intrinsic is fixed at −3.00 (= 100P − 2·95P + 87P intrinsics), so max loss = −3.00 + 0.45 = −$2.55 → −$255. The single (downside) breakeven sits at 89.55: between body and broken wing the P&L line is S − 90 + 0.45, which crosses zero at S = 89.55 (e.g. at spot 92 it is still +$245, not a loss). Numbers shown are illustrative at 30% IV, 35 DTE; exact credit, max loss and breakeven depend on live skew — verify on your chain.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Net Δ: this put BWB is mildly bullish (positive delta) — you want spot to drift up toward / hold above the body and let the structure decay into the credit. Γ: net SHORT gamma near the body (like any short fly tent) but with a long-gamma "kicker" out on the broken wing — that asymmetry is the convexity you bought cheaply. Θ: positive when spot sits between body and the near wing (you collect decay), negative if spot is parked under the body inside the tent peak. V: small and sign-flips with spot location — roughly short vega near the body, long vega out near the far wing. The honest read: it is a defined-risk, low-net-Greek position whose edge is geometric (the skipped strike), not a clean directional or vol bet.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: a neutral-to-mildly-directional view (here mildly bullish via a put BWB placed below spot) in a moderate-to-high IVR regime, no binary event inside the DTE. You want IV elevated enough that the skipped far wing is cheap (skew helps put BWBs) so the credit is real, plus an expectation that spot grinds rather than gaps. It WORKS when spot expires at/above the body: keep credit on the no-risk side or bank the tent peak. It FAILS when spot crashes through the broken wing into the defined-loss zone, OR when you misprice the skew and pay a debit you thought was a credit. It is NOT a "free money" trade — the "no risk to one side" is paid for by accepting a wider, real loss on the other.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Mechanism is well-documented: the put BWB is explicitly described as "an advanced bullish strategy with the goal of having NO upside risk," entered for a net credit by skipping a lower strike to buy a cheaper OTM put. Illustrative reconstruction (marked est.): on a ~$100 underlying at IVR ~50 with normal put skew, a 100/95/87 put BWB might come in for a ~$0.30–$0.60 net credit per spread with a ~$2.40–$2.70 broken-side max loss — i.e. you risk ~$255 to keep ~$45 credit with no upside risk, profiting most if spot pins ~95. The lesson: the credit is small and the tail loss is multiples larger, so a BWB is a distribution bet (high win-rate, asymmetric loss), not a windfall — exactly the convexity/negative-skew lesson from F2.

Sources: Fidelity Learning Center; tastytrade / Option Alpha BWB guides; TD/Schwab Ticker Tape "Turning a Long Butterfly Spread into a Credit Spread" (concept, not live quotes).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
Entry IVR gate> 40 (skew makes the far wing cheap)
Delta / strikeBody short strikes ~16–25Δ; near wing 5 wide; far wing skip +1 strike (~8 wide)
DTE window30–45 DTE; manage by 21 DTE
Profit target25–50% of max profit, or any time you can close for ≥ entry credit risk-free
Stop / time-stopClose if spot breaches the broken wing; hard time-stop at 21 DTE (gamma)
SizingDefined-risk: max loss (broken side) ≤ 1–2% of account per position
Defend triggerActionResulting structureNew risk
Spot drifts toward body (winning)Take 25–50% profit, do not get greedy near the tent peakFlat / freed capitalOpportunity cost only
Spot falls toward near short strikesRoll the untested far wing in to re-center, or closeRe-centered BWB / closedPay a debit to roll; narrows credit cushion
Spot breaching broken wingClose the whole package — do not "hope" through the wide sideRealized defined lossNone (loss booked); avoids pin/assignment at expiry
Worked trade ticket — Underlying XYZ @ $100.00, IVR 52. Put BWB: +1 Aug 100P @ 3.80 / −2 Aug 95P @ 2.20 / +1 Aug 87P @ 0.15, 35 DTE, 1 spread. Net credit = 2·2.20 − 3.80 − 0.15 = +$0.45 ($45). Max profit (spot at body 95) = peak intrinsic 5.00 + credit 0.45 = $5.45 ($545). Max loss (below 87) = broken-side intrinsic −3.00 + credit 0.45 = −$2.55 ($255). BPR ≈ $255 (defined-risk = max loss). Breakevens: upside = none (keep credit above 100); downside = $89.55 (= body 95 − near-wing 5 + credit 0.45). Target: close at 25–50% of peak or for a small debit/flat once de-risked. Stop: spot < $89.55 (at/through the downside breakeven, approaching the broken wing). Roll trigger: spot ≤ $95 with >21 DTE → roll far wing up. Exit: 21 DTE hard time-stop. (All figures est./illustrative — confirm on live chain.)

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen: liquid optionable names, per-leg OI > 500 and bid/ask < 10% of mid (a 4-leg trade pays the spread FOUR times — slippage is the real enemy), IVR > 40 with visible put skew, share price $40–$300 so 5-wide strikes are meaningful, and NO earnings inside the DTE. Use the tastytrade / Option Alpha chain or the FREE Barchart options screener (filter by IV Rank and OI). Candidate as-of 2026-06-17 (mark est., verify IVR live): broad-index proxies like SPY/IWM (deep liquidity, tight 1-wide strikes, well-defined skew for put BWBs) and a liquid single name such as XLE when energy IVR is elevated — pick whichever currently shows IVR > 40 with no event risk.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: the "no risk on one side / opened for a credit" headline seduces beginners into treating the broken wing as harmless. It is not — the broken side carries a defined loss that here is ~5.7x the credit ($255 loss vs $45 credit), and a single gap through it erases many winners (classic negative-skew distribution). Second killer: four legs of bid/ask on illiquid names quietly turn your "credit" into a debit at the mid you can actually fill. Third: pin risk if spot sits at a body strike into expiry — close before expiry, never hold for the last cent.

Honest edge — the falsifiable metric is the realized win-rate vs. the implied breakeven win-rate over ≥30 logged trades, NET of all four bid/ask spreads. If your fill-quality is tight and your skew read is right you harvest a small structural edge; if not, costs eat it. Verdict: Skill-dependent — geometry gives a real (small) edge only to disciplined, fill-conscious traders; sloppy execution makes it Crowded-low-edge.

L2 · Intermediate

PMCC / LEAPS & Diagonals 窮人掩護性買權／長天期對角價差

Replace 100 shares with a deep-ITM long-dated call and sell short-dated calls against it — a capital-efficient synthetic covered call that trades cash for early-exercise, rate and vega risk.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Covered Call · Parity & synthetics · Mechanics, assignment & pin · Rho / rates / dividends

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: a Poor Man's Covered Call (PMCC) is a long call diagonal that replicates a covered call without owning 100 shares. You buy a deep-ITM LEAPS call (≈ 0.80–0.90 delta, 9–24 months out) as a stock surrogate — at that delta it moves nearly dollar-for-dollar with the stock — then sell a shorter-dated OTM call (≈ 0.20–0.35 delta, 30–45 DTE) against it, collecting premium just like a covered call. "Long stock" is replaced by a high-delta long call, so you put up a fraction of the capital → synthetic leverage. Critically, the parity link (F3) is NOT exact here: unlike a real covered call, you do NOT own shares, you do NOT receive the dividend, and your long LEAPS carries extrinsic value, vega and rho that shares don't. So a PMCC is a covered call's payoff shape bolted onto a long-call's Greek profile — capital-efficient, but with rate/vega/early-exercise dependencies that a plain CC never has.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

PMCC (poor man's covered call) payoff: a deep-ITM long LEAPS call (high delta) financed partly by a short near-dated OTM call. The teal curve sits on a flat max-loss floor at the net debit below the long strike, climbs with a stock-like slope (high net delta) through breakeven, then flattens into a capped max profit above the short strike — replicating a covered call at a fraction of the 100-share capital. The gold callout flags ex-dividend early-assignment risk: if the short call goes ITM near an ex-div date, it can be assigned/called away early.
≈ (short K − long K) − net debitmax profit (if short called away)
net debit paidmax loss (LEAPS → 0)
long K + net debitapprox breakeven (front expiry)

Multi-expiry — the engine cannot draw this (it renders a single expiry only). Before the SHORT call expires, P&L is a path/IV-dependent curve, not a clean kinked payoff: the long LEAPS still has time value, so a rally lifts the LEAPS more than it hurts the short call up to the short strike, then the position caps. The hand-drawn SVG shows the curved, time-and-IV-dependent profile (T-now) sitting under the eventual expiry kink. Max loss ≈ the net debit (if the underlying collapses and the LEAPS decays toward intrinsic→0). Max profit per cycle ≈ the short premium collected; over the life, ≈ (short strike − long strike) − total net debit, IF the underlying finishes at the short strike and you manage rolls well. Numbers depend on the specific LEAPS debit and skew — verify live.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Net Δ: POSITIVE and dominant ≈ long LEAPS delta (0.80–0.90) minus short call delta (0.25–0.35) → net ~+0.50–0.65 per spread. Like a covered call (L1-2), its dominant risk is long directional delta, not a vol bet — it loses in a real selloff. Γ — STATE/SPOT-DEPENDENT, do NOT call this "small net long": net gamma is the deep-ITM LEAPS' tiny gamma MINUS the short front call's gamma, and the short front gamma is a moving target. While spot sits well below the short strike, the short call is far OTM, its gamma is small, and the position is mildly net LONG gamma. But as spot RISES toward the short strike, the short front call's gamma SPIKES (gamma peaks ATM and is largest on the near-dated leg), so it dominates the LEAPS' low gamma and the position flips NET SHORT gamma — its net delta now FALLS as spot rises (the cap engaging). That short-gamma region is exactly where the structure is most fragile. Θ: the short front call decays FASTER than the long LEAPS (front theta > back theta per day), so net theta is mildly positive while spot sits below the short strike — but in the net-short-gamma zone you are short gamma / long theta, the classic "picking up pennies" exposure. V: net LONG vega (the LEAPS has far more vega than the short front) — a vol spike helps the LEAPS, a vol crush hurts; this is the BIG difference from a real covered call, which is short-vol-ish. ρ: positive and non-trivial — a long-dated call gains value as rates rise (carry), so PMCCs have real rate sensitivity that shares don't.

Note: the engine plots single-expiry Greeks, so the curve above shows the SHORT front call's theta only (the harvested side — positive theta on the short leg). That same front leg is also where the danger lives: its gamma is largest near its strike, so as spot approaches 108 the short-front gamma dominates and the FULL position turns net SHORT gamma. The long LEAPS adds steady long delta + long vega the engine can't render across two expiries, but its gamma is far too small to offset the front near the cap.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: a moderately BULLISH view (you want a slow grind up, not a moonshot) on a quality, low-dividend, liquid underlying, entered when the LEAPS IV is reasonable (lowish IVR on the long-dated tenor) so you don't overpay vega, while the front you sell still carries decent premium. It WORKS in a steady up-drift: the LEAPS appreciates and you repeatedly harvest front premium, far cheaper than buying 100 shares. It FAILS in (a) a big selloff — net long delta + you paid a debit, so you bleed; (b) a violent rally past the short strike — capped, and worse, the LEAPS' time value can lag if IV crushes on the way up; (c) high-dividend names — early assignment risk (facet ⑧). Do NOT run it as a delta-neutral income trade; it is leveraged long equity with a covered-call cap.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Mechanism documented by Option Alpha, IG, tastytrade and MoneyShow ("Analyzing LEAPS for the Poor Man's Covered Call"): buy a deep-ITM LEAPS (0.80+ delta, behaves "almost identically to owning shares"), sell a ~0.28-delta shorter-dated call. Illustrative reconstruction on a ~$200 large-cap (marked est.): a 12-month 160-strike LEAPS (~0.85Δ) might cost ~$50 ($5,000) vs ~$20,000 for 100 shares — a ~4x capital saving — while selling a 35-DTE 220 call brings ~$3 ($300, ~6% of the LEAPS cost per cycle). The documented LESSON across sources: the strategy lives or dies on the short call NOT being assigned around ex-dividend and on the LEAPS retaining time value — and because you don't get the dividend, PMCC on a high-yield name structurally underperforms a real covered call. Premium figures are reconstructed est.; confirm on a live chain.

Sources: tastytrade, "Poor Man's Covered Call"; MoneyShow, "Analyzing LEAPS for the Poor Man's Covered Call."

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen: liquid LEAPS market (long-dated OI > 200, bid/ask < 5% of mid on the LEAPS — wide LEAPS spreads silently destroy the edge), low or zero dividend (avoid early-exercise drama), share price $50–$400, steady uptrend / quality balance sheet, LEAPS-tenor IVR < 50, and weekly/monthly fronts to sell. Use the FREE Barchart or Market Chameleon screens (filter long-dated OI + IV Rank) and your broker's chain. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (mark est., verify dividend dates + IVR live): a low-yield quality megacap such as GOOGL or AMZN (no/low dividend → minimal ex-div assignment risk, deep LEAPS liquidity) over a high-yield name like a utility or bank where dividend capture wrecks the short call.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The real blow-up is NOT "leveraged covered-call carry" — it is a RIP-THROUGH-THE-SHORT-STRIKE squeeze. When the stock rips up TO and THROUGH the short front strike, two things compound. First, you are now net SHORT gamma at the worst spot (facet ③): the short front call's gamma is largest near its strike, so its delta accelerates toward −1 fast — its losses accelerate. Second, your long deep-ITM LEAPS is already near +0.85–0.90 delta with little gamma left to give, so its gains LAG the short call's accelerating losses. The cap doesn't just engage smoothly; the short leg's loss out-runs the LEAPS' gain through the transition, and a fast gap can leave you net negative on the day even though you are "bullish and capped." Now layer the gotcha that turns a squeeze into a disaster: early assignment of the short call around ex-dividend. Per the dividend-exercise rule (CBOE / Schwab), if the short call's remaining EXTRINSIC value is less than the upcoming dividend, the long holder will rationally exercise to capture it — most likely precisely when the call is ITM after a rip — and because you own NO shares, you get assigned −100 shares and must scramble (exercise the LEAPS or buy to cover), often at a loss and with a margin call. Then the chronic, slower risks: it is leveraged LONG DELTA (a hard selloff hits both your debit and your thesis); it is LONG VEGA (a vol crush, even on a rally, deflates the LEAPS); and it is LONG RHO (falling rates erode long-dated call value). The "synthetic covered call" label hides the spot-dependent short-gamma + LEAPS-delta-lag + ex-div assignment combination; it is NOT parity-equivalent to a real CC.

Honest edge — the falsifiable metric: over ≥20 cycles, does your realized return on the net debit beat simply holding the high-delta LEAPS alone (the short-call premium net of roll costs and any assignment damage)? If yes, the diagonal adds carry; if the short keeps getting run over or assigned, you'd have been better off without it. Verdict: Skill-dependent — genuine capital-efficiency edge on quality low-div names with disciplined ex-div management; turns Crowded-low-edge / negative on high-div names or sloppy rolling.

L2 · Intermediate

Calendars (Time Spreads) 日曆價差（時間價差）

Sell a near-dated option and buy a longer-dated one at the same strike — a LONG-VEGA, SHORT-GAMMA bet on the term structure of implied vol, whose worst enemy is a large spot move.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: IV & the surface · The Greeks · Diagonals · bridges to L3-5 Vol term structure

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down — and getting this framing exactly right matters: a long calendar = SELL the near-dated option, BUY the longer-dated option, SAME strike (calls or puts; payoff is similar at the strike). It is NOT a delta-range income trade like an iron condor. It is a term-structure bet wrapped in a time-decay engine: the near option you sold decays FASTER (per-day theta is higher the closer to expiry) than the back option you own, so if spot pins the strike you collect the differential decay. But the position's defining exposures are vega and gamma: you are LONG VEGA (the back-month option has far more vega than the front you sold → you profit if IV rises or the term structure steepens) and you are SHORT GAMMA near the strike (you sold the high-gamma front; a big move makes the front move against you faster than the back protects). One more critical distinction (L3-5): the curve a calendar trades is the SPX/single-name OPTIONS implied-vol term structure (ATM IV across option expirations) — this is NOT the VIX-FUTURES term structure (the VX roll / ETP path) and NOT tradable VIX spot. A calendar is a clean retail expression of "spot will sit near the strike, and the back-month IV will hold" — collected EITHER via the theta differential on a normal upward curve (Setup A, where the near you sell is the CHEAPER leg) OR via a front IV crush when the near is rich into an event (Setup B). See facet ④.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Long-calendar P&L at front-month expiry: a tent peaked at the strike (max profit when the underlying pins K and the back-month leg retains its time value), sloping into capped losses (the net debit) on a large move in either direction. The position is long vega / short gamma, so its real value is path- and IV-dependent — a rally or selloff hurts, and an IV crush in the back leg flattens the tent.

Chart is a SINGLE-EXPIRY Black-Scholes approximation of an inherently MULTI-EXPIRY structure — it sketches the tent shape at front expiry only; the true P&L is path- and IV-dependent and cannot be drawn as one static line.

peak at strike (front expiry)max profit (tent peaks AT the strike)
net debit paidmax loss (large move either way)
two breakevens around Knarrow profit band, IV-dependent

Multi-expiry — the engine cannot draw this (single expiry only), and that limitation is the whole point: a calendar has NO clean expiry payoff line before the front expires. The hand-drawn SVG shows the path-/IV-dependent tent: profit PEAKS when spot sits AT the strike at FRONT expiry, falls off on either side (the short-gamma wings), and the entire tent rises if back-month IV expands or falls if it crushes. P&L is genuinely path- and IV-dependent, not a fixed kink. Max loss = the net debit (a large move in EITHER direction). Breakevens are two points bracketing the strike whose location moves with implied vol — you cannot read them off a static expiry diagram, which is exactly why beginners misjudge this trade.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Net Δ: ~0 at entry if struck ATM (delta-neutral-ish), but it does NOT stay neutral — as spot drifts off the strike, delta builds against you. Γ: SHORT — this is the load-bearing exposure. You sold the high-gamma front, so a large, fast spot move is the worst enemy (the front explodes faster than the back cushions). Θ: positive while spot sits near the strike (front decays faster than back). V: LONG — the dominant edge/risk. The back-month carries more vega than the front, so net vega is positive: a rise in (back-month) IV inflates the tent; an IV crush deflates it even if spot behaves. The honest one-liner: a calendar is long vega + short gamma + positive theta near the strike — you are paid theta to be short gamma, and you own vega as a directional bet on the term structure.

Note: the engine renders single-expiry Greeks only — shown is the SHORT FRONT call's gamma (negative, peaking at the strike: your short-gamma risk). The long back-month adds the offsetting long vega + slower decay the engine can't plot across two expiries.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: a neutral / pin view on spot (you expect it to sit near the strike) combined with a term-structure view. There are TWO clearly different setups — they share the same structure (sell near, buy far, same strike; always long-vega, short-gamma, positive-theta near the strike) but make money for different reasons. Get them straight or you will mislabel the edge.

SETUP A — Normal market (UPWARD options IV term structure: near IV < far IV). This is the default regime. The near option you SOLD is the CHEAPER IV, the far you BOUGHT is the richer-vega leg. Your edge is the THETA DIFFERENTIAL: the near decays faster per day than the far, so if spot pins the strike you collect the differential decay. You have NO vol-term edge here — you are paying up for the far's vega, not selling a rich front — so the trade leans entirely on the underlying PINNING near the strike (a small realized move) AND on back-month IV not falling. Do NOT call this "sell the rich near": on an upward curve the near is the cheaper leg. Edge = skill-dependent.

SETUP B — Front-rich / event (FLAT or BACKWARDATED front: near IV ≥ far IV, e.g. into earnings). Now the front IV is elevated/rich and the back is the cheaper leg. Here — and ONLY here — you genuinely SELL the rich near and BUY the cheaper far. You profit from the post-event IV CRUSH of the front (it collapses after the catalyst) while the far holds, provided the underlying stays near the strike. The danger is the same short-gamma exposure: a large event move overwhelms the crush gain, so an earnings calendar is a bet that the realized post-event move is SMALLER than the implied (expected) move — not a free crush trade.

Either setup WORKS when spot stays near the strike into front expiry AND the back-month IV holds. Either FAILS on a LARGE spot move in either direction (short gamma — the single biggest killer) OR on a back-month IV crush (long vega) even if spot pins. It is NOT a wide delta-range condor. (This matches L3-5: standard calendar on the upward curve = theta-differential + long vega; the "sell the rich front" framing belongs only to the flat/inverted-front event variant.)

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Dated term-structure episode (verified): in early August 2024, after the Aug-5 volatility spike, the VIX-FUTURES curve flipped into deep backwardation — per Harbourfronts' study, VX futures were in backwardation ~53% of the time from Aug 1–Nov 4 2024 vs only ~7% historically (Jan-2013–Jul-2024). The SPX-OPTIONS IV term structure inverted the same way (front IV > back IV). The LESSON for calendars: a front-rich / backwardated options curve is the SETUP-B (event) regime — when near IV > far IV you genuinely SELL the rich near and BUY the cheaper far, exactly what the event variant wants — BUT it usually comes with violent realized moves, and the worst enemy here is the short-gamma whipsaw (the Aug-2024 spot move), NOT the term-structure shape; the textbook discipline is tight move-stops or standing aside in chaos, not flipping to a reverse calendar. (Contrast Setup A — the normal upward curve, near IV < far IV — where the edge is the theta differential and you are buying the richer far, not selling a rich front.) The contrasting earnings case (well-documented by tastytrade/MenthorQ): pre-earnings the front-month IV is bid 10–15+ points above the back (near rich / backwardated) — the canonical Setup-B IV-crush calendar of selling the rich near — but the post-earnings GAP is the short-gamma risk that can still erase it. Note carefully: VIX-futures backwardation (above) is NOT the same curve a single-name calendar trades — it's the macro signal that contango/backwardation regimes flip. Magnitudes are dated/sourced; treat the IV-differential figures as est.

Sources: Harbourfront Technologies — "VIX Futures Backwardation Aug–Nov 2024" study; tastytrade / MenthorQ — pre-earnings front-vs-back IV and calendar mechanics.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ATM long call calendar: −1 30-DTE 100C (front, ~50Δ, sold) / +1 60-DTE 100C (back, ~50Δ, bought), 1 spread. Net debit ≈ $1.50 ($150) = max loss / BPR (defined). Ticket B — Front-rich / event IV-crush calendar (sell the elevated front) — Underlying QQQ @ $100.00 (normalized), into a known earnings/catalyst. Profit target: +25–40% (~$225–$250), realized typically the morning AFTER the report. Stop / exit: exit right after the crush (do not hold for residual theta); cut if the post-event spot gap exceeds the ±$4 expected move (short gamma overwhelms the crush). Never hold the short front to expiry.

Both tickets normalized/illustrative est. — confirm on a real chain; QQQ used for liquidity. Consistent with L3-5 and the master decision table.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen: high-liquidity, multi-expiry chains (weekly + monthly back-months both with OI > 1,000, bid/ask < 5% of mid on BOTH legs — calendars are vega-sensitive so wide back-month spreads ruin the fill), a readable term structure (compare ATM IV across expirations) — for Setup A a normal UPWARD curve (near IV < far IV: lean on the theta differential + a tight pin), or for Setup B a flat/backwardated front (near IV ≥ far IV, into an event: sell the rich near for the IV crush) — and, for the non-event Setup A, NO earnings between front and back expiry. Use the FREE Barchart "Volatility Term Structure" chart (per ticker) plus Market Chameleon for the IV-by-expiry table; vixcentral.com for the MACRO VIX-futures curve regime (contango vs backwardation) as a top-down filter. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (mark est., verify term structure live): index ETFs SPY/QQQ (deepest multi-expiry liquidity, clean ATM IV term structure, cash-pin-friendly) for either regime; for an earnings calendar, a liquid single name reporting soon (e.g. a megacap with weeklies) where front IV ≥ back +10–15 vol pts (the canonical front-rich setup).

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha (correction #4): beginners file the calendar next to the iron condor as a "neutral income / range" trade and size it like one — then a single large move blows the short-gamma tent apart. A LARGE spot move in EITHER direction is the worst enemy, not a benign range exit. Second killer: it is LONG VEGA, so a back-month IV CRUSH loses money even if spot pins perfectly — many "the stock didn't move, why did I lose?" calendars die here. Third: confusing the two term structures — the VIX-FUTURES curve (VX roll / ETP) is NOT the OPTIONS IV term structure your calendar trades, and VIX spot isn't tradable; reading the wrong curve picks the wrong regime. Fourth: holding the short front into expiry → pin/assignment risk on the leg you sold.

Honest edge — the falsifiable metric: over ≥20 trades, does your realized P&L correlate with the term-structure differential you entered on (front-minus-back IV at entry) AND survive your short-gamma move-stops? Edge exists only if (a) you reliably enter when the OPTIONS term structure is favorable and (b) you cut large moves fast. Verdict: Skill-dependent — a genuine term-structure/vega edge for traders who read the right curve and respect short gamma; degrades to Crowded-low-edge for anyone treating it as a set-and-forget range trade.

Trade now (small, disciplined)

Defined-risk short-premium/VRP in high IVR; earnings IV-crush via defined-risk structures; simple term-structure tilts.

Practice / observe

Delta-neutral & gamma scalping — skill-dependent; transaction costs and discrete-hedge error eat most of the VRP for retail.

Avoid

Naked short straddles / undefined short vol; trading skew without parity (F3) and the second-order Greeks (L3-8).

L3 · Advanced

Variance vs Volatility & the VRP 變異數與波動率·波動率風險溢酬

The structural reason vol-selling pays: implied variance is, on average, priced ABOVE the variance that later realizes — and variance (not vol) is the quantity options actually trade.

Genuine (defined-risk)
Prereq: Greeks (Γ, V) · IV & realized vol · parity/synthetics

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: an option's value is driven by the variance it will accumulate (σ²·t), not by volatility (σ) directly. Variance is the additive, tradable quantity — daily squared returns sum linearly; vol (its square root) does not. That is why a variance swap, not a "vol swap," is the clean instrument, and why a delta-hedged option's payoff is a bet on Σ(ΔS/S)² versus the implied σ²·t baked into its price. The Variance Risk Premium (VRP) is the wedge: implied variance − subsequently realized variance > 0 on average for equity indices. In vol-point shorthand, VIX (≈ 30-day implied σ on SPX) tends to sit ~2–4 points above the realized σ that follows (est., regime-dependent; widens in calm, collapses or inverts in a crash). You are paid this wedge for warehousing the risk that realized blows past implied — i.e. for being short the convexity (short gamma) that protects everyone else. One guard: VIX is an index of SPX-options implied vol — it is not itself tradable spot, and the VRP here is the implied-vs-realized wedge on the SAME underlying, measured at a single horizon — NOT a futures-roll or term-structure trade.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Variance risk premium mechanics: implied vol (gold) sits consistently above realized vol (blue), and that persistent gap (teal shading) is the premium a vol-seller harvests bar by bar. The catch is the left-tail event — realized spikes through implied (red), handing back months of accumulated carry in a single move. Net: selling vol is positive-expectancy but negatively skewed; the tail is the price you pay for the steady carry.

This is a concept module, not a single structure, so there is no clean expiry payoff. The mechanics are best read as two distributions on the SAME underlying and horizon: the implied variance you sell (a single number, σ_impl²·t, locked at entry) versus the realized variance that accrues path-by-path, Σ(ΔS/S)². Empirically the implied-variance distribution sits to the RIGHT of the realized-variance distribution — implied > realized most of the time — and that persistent rightward offset IS the premium: index buyers overpay for crash insurance, so the seller collects σ_impl² − σ_real² as carry. The economic payoff of harvesting it is approximately ½·|Γ|·[(σ_impl²) − (σ_real²)]·S²·t per unit time, where |Γ| is the (negative-gamma) position's gamma magnitude: positive when realized variance undershoots implied. But the same |Γ| works against you in the LEFT tail — when a gap drives realized variance far above the implied you sold, that single term turns deeply negative and gives back many months of carry in days. So selling vol earns a real, recurring carry, but the return stream is negatively skewed: many small wins, occasional large short-gamma losses. The retail-correct way to express this edge is a defined-risk structure (iron condor / iron fly) that keeps the short-gamma carry but BUYS back the far-left tail with long wings — converting "undefined gap risk" into a known, sized max loss. The breakeven is the realized-vol level that exactly equals the implied vol you sold — the breakeven vol, not a breakeven price.

σ_impl² > σ_real²implied variance > realized → the premium (carry)
~2–4 ptsavg SPX VRP in vol-point terms (est., normal regime)
≤ 0left tail: realized variance overshoots → short-γ loss

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

The VRP itself is a statistical edge, not a position, but the cleanest vehicle for it (short variance, whether a defined-risk iron fly/condor or an undefined-risk delta-hedged straddle) is: Δ ≈ 0 (hedged or symmetric), Γ < 0 (the risk you are paid for — realized-vol/gamma risk), Θ > 0 (the daily carry that IS the premium), V < 0 (separable — you can hedge vega with another option and stay short gamma; see VRP harvesting). The crucial decomposition (error #5): the premium compensates SHORT GAMMA — the path-by-path realized-vol risk — while vega is a distinct, often-hedgeable exposure. You can be near vega-neutral and still be earning (and risking) the VRP through gamma. Concretely, the short-gamma P&L each step is ≈ −½·|Γ|·(ΔS)² offset by +Θ: while realized moves stay small the theta carry wins; in a left-tail gap the (ΔS)² term explodes — but in a defined-risk iron fly/condor the long wings cap that explosion, which is exactly why the defined-risk version is the retail default.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: flat-to-orderly tape × elevated-or-fair IVR × no fat-tail catalyst. The VRP is genuine and persistent on broad indices (SPX/RUT) where the demand for crash insurance is structural — that structural overpayment is exactly why implied variance averages above realized. It fails, violently, in the exact regime that defines the tail: a gap (Aug 5 2024, Feb 2018, Mar 2020), when realized variance over a few sessions dwarfs the implied you sold and the short-gamma term gives back the year's carry. For a NAKED short-vol seller that tail is open-ended; for a DEFINED-RISK iron fly/condor seller it is capped at the (known) max loss — the difference between a bad month and a blown account. Note the trap: high IVR tells you implied is rich versus its own history — it does NOT tell you implied is rich versus forward realized. IVR alone is not edge; the VRP is the average implied-minus-realized gap, and the average hides a left tail that "eats the year."

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

SPX, 2024 full year (as-of 2026-06-17). 2024 realized vol on SPX was among the lowest in three years, yet VIX averaged a clear premium over it for most of the year — the textbook VRP, implied variance sitting above realized, paid to sellers month after month. Then the tail: on Aug 5, 2024, VIX printed an intraday high of 65.73 in thin pre-market trade (the largest one-day spike in its history) before closing at 38.57 — yet that month's realized vol settled near 19–20% (est.). Three lessons: (1) the implied spike massively overstated what realized — the VRP re-asserted within days, rewarding sellers who survived; (2) a NAKED seller who was margin-called or stopped out at the 65 print locked an open-ended short-gamma loss before the premium could pay back; (3) a DEFINED-RISK iron condor seller's loss that day was capped at width-minus-credit no matter how high VIX printed — they could not be force-liquidated beyond their max loss. The yen-carry unwind was a driver, not the sole cause; illiquid pre-market SPX options widened spreads and inflated the VIX print itself. Sources: CME, BIS Bulletin 95, Macroption (verified ≥2; intraday/indicative high, not close).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked trade ticket — DEFINED-RISK (retail default, illustrative est.) — SPY iron condor to harvest the VRP with a KNOWN max loss. Underlying SPY ≈ 540, IVR ≈ 40, 45 DTE. Sell the 16Δ strangle and buy $5-wide wings on each side: short 520 put / long 515 put · short 560 call / long 565 call. Net credit ≈ $1.55 (×100 = $155 per condor). Max loss = width − credit = $5.00 − $1.55 = $3.45 (×100 = $345), reached only beyond a wing. BPR ≈ max loss ≈ $345 per condor (defined-risk margin = the long-wing protection, NOT SPAN). POP ≈ ~70% (two 16Δ shorts → ≈ 1 − 0.16 − 0.16 ≈ 68%, before management); managing at 50% credit raises realized win-rate further. Breakeven vol ≈ the sold IV; profit if 45-day realized comes in below it. Target: close at 50% credit (~$78); Stop: loss = 2× credit (~$310) OR realized vol > 1.5× sold IV; Roll: 21 DTE; Exit: target, stop, or any fat-tail catalyst. Size so one condor's $345 max loss is ≤ 1–2% of the account; the worst case is fully known BEFORE entry, with NO gap or margin-call risk beyond it. (SPX equivalent: same 16Δ / 5-wide condor on SPX is 10× the notional and cash-settled — use only when account size makes the per-trade max loss still ≤ 1–2%.)
Advanced note — NAKED / delta-hedged short straddle (PRO · PAPER-ONLY · not the default) — the same VRP edge expressed with UNDEFINED risk. SPX ≈ 5400, IVR ≈ 40, sell 45-DTE ATM 5400 straddle for ~210.00 credit (×100 = $21,000) at sold IV ≈ 14%; delta-hedge with SPX futures/SPY to keep net Δ ≈ 0. (Sanity check: ATM 45-DTE straddle ≈ 0.8·S·σ·√t ≈ 0.8 × 5400 × 0.14 × √(45/365) ≈ 210.) Max loss: UNDEFINED (open-ended gap risk); BPR ≈ $45,000 (SPAN); requires active intraday delta-hedging — all pro-level. This version has a strictly worse left tail than the condor above: an overnight gap can lose multiples of the credit AND trigger a margin call before the VRP pays back (see Aug 5 2024). Treat it as a learning/paper exercise to FEEL short gamma — not as a trade-from-this for a retail account.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Harvest the VRP only where it is structural and liquid: SPX / SPY / RUT / NDX index complex (deep demand for crash hedges = persistent premium). Screen: option OI > 5,000 at target strikes, bid/ask < 5% of mid, IVR > 30, NO earnings/FOMC/CPI inside DTE, beta-stable underlying. For retail, prefer SPY for the defined-risk condor (small per-contract notional → easy to size max loss at 1–2%). Free tools: Cboe.com (VIX vs SPX realized), barchart.com (IV vs HV overlay), marketchameleon.com (IV/HV ratio screener, free tier). Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): SPY for the cleanest retail-sized VRP, SPX for cash-settlement at larger size; avoid single names where the "premium" is mostly event risk, not VRP.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: the VRP is an average over a brutally left-skewed distribution. You collect 2–4 vol points of implied-over-realized carry for months, then one gap (Aug 5 '24, Feb 5 '18) where realized variance overshoots implied gives back a year through short gamma. In a NAKED short-vol position that tail is open-ended and account-ending; in a DEFINED-RISK iron condor/fly the long wings cap it at a pre-known max loss — which is the whole point of using the defined-risk expression for retail. Small samples over-state the edge because the left tail hasn't shown up yet. And the premium is for short GAMMA — selling vega without delta-hedging muddles two different exposures and mis-attributes your P&L.

Honest edge — falsifiable metric: a multi-year, after-cost backtest of (sold IV − subsequent realized) on the SAME underlying. If that median stays positive net of slippage AND the worst-drawdown is survivable at your sizing, the edge is real. Verdict: VRP harvesting via DEFINED-RISK structures (iron condor / iron fly) = Genuine-but-modest for retail — a real, recurring carry whose max loss is known and sizeable to 1–2% per trade. NAKED short vol (short straddle / strangle, even delta-hedged) = pro / paper-only: the negatively-skewed tail is open-ended and eats accounts; the edge is the same but the risk is uncontrolled. Either way it degrades to Skill-dependent the moment you over-lever.

L3 · Advanced

Delta-Neutral Construction Delta中性

Strip out the directional bet so the position's P&L is driven by the Greeks you actually want (Γ, V, Θ) — not by where the underlying drifts.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Position Greeks · VRP · execution (the hedge costs)

① 機制 Mechanism

Plain first: "delta-neutral" means net position delta ≈ 0, so a small move in the underlying causes (to first order) no P&L. You build it by offsetting option delta with the underlying — long an ATM straddle (Δ ≈ 0 already) or sell shares/futures against a directional option book. The point is isolation: with delta zeroed, what remains is your real bet — gamma (do moves help or hurt?), vega (does IV rise or fall?), theta (time carry). It is the construction layer beneath gamma scalping (L3-3) and VRP harvesting (L3-1). Critical nuance: neutral is instantaneous and first-order only. As spot moves, gamma re-introduces delta (delta drifts non-zero); as time passes, charm shifts it; as IV moves, vanna shifts it. "Neutral" is a snapshot you must keep re-establishing — that re-balancing IS the trade.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

The payoff shown is a long ATM straddle — the canonical delta-neutral starting point: net Δ ≈ 0 at the money, symmetric V-shape, profits on a move in EITHER direction. Once you delta-hedge it with stock, the expiry payoff is no longer the picture; the relevant P&L becomes the gamma-vs-theta race (next facet). Breakevens of the un-hedged straddle: spot ± total premium. The "breakeven" of the hedged version is realized vol = implied vol.

≈ 0net delta at ATM
S ± premiumun-hedged breakevens
σ_real = σ_implhedged breakeven

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

By design Δ ≈ 0 (the whole point). The RESIDUAL Greeks are what you live with: for a long straddle, Γ > 0 (large near ATM — delta swings fast as spot moves, the engine of scalping), Θ < 0 (you bleed time), V > 0 (gain if IV rises). The delta plot below shows why neutral is fragile: at ATM net delta crosses zero, but it slopes steeply through it — a small spot move pushes delta sharply positive or negative (that slope IS gamma). The further spot travels, the bigger the un-hedged delta you've re-accumulated, which is exactly what you periodically trade out (gamma scalp) or what bleeds you if realized stays low.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: you have a view on VOLATILITY (or a Greek), NOT on direction. Use it when you want pure long-gamma (expect realized > implied), pure short-gamma (VRP harvest), pure vega (term-structure / calendar), or to immunize a book's directional drift while you trade something else. It FAILS as a "set and forget" — neutral decays the instant the market moves, and re-hedging has a cost (spread + commission + slippage) that is certain while the gamma payoff is not. It also fails if you confuse delta-neutral with risk-neutral: a delta-neutral book can still be torpedoed by a vega or gamma spike.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Aug 5, 2024 (SPX), as-of 2026-06-17. A trader long an ATM SPX straddle and delta-hedging into that morning had a textbook lesson in residual-Greek behavior. With VIX gapping to an intraday 65.73 and SPX futures down ~10% off the July peak, the long-gamma book's delta swung hard and fast — re-hedging the explosive delta as spot fell locked in genuine gamma profits (realized vol that morning was enormous), AND the long vega exploded as IV tripled. The mirror image — a delta-neutral SHORT straddle — saw the same two residuals turn lethal: short gamma forced buying into the fall, short vega bled as IV ripped. Same "neutral" construction, opposite sign, opposite outcome — proof that delta-neutral defines what you are NOT betting on, not whether you are safe. Sources: CME, BIS Bulletin 95, Macroption (verified ≥2; intraday/indicative high, not close).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked trade ticket (illustrative, reconstructed est.) — Delta-neutral long straddle on a normalized $100 underlying. Buy 1× 30-DTE 100 call + 1× 100 put, IV 30%, net debit ≈ $6.85/share ($685). Initial net Δ ≈ 0 (call +0.52, put −0.48, est.). Γ ≈ +0.046/share, Θ ≈ −$11/day, V ≈ +$11/1 vol pt (×100, est.). Re-hedge band: trade ~20 shares of stock whenever the option leg's net delta drifts past ±0.20/share (≈ 20 position deltas, roughly a ±$4 move). Max loss (un-hedged): $685 if pinned at 100 to expiry; with hedging, loss ≈ accumulated theta minus gamma scalps. BPR ≈ debit. Breakevens (un-hedged): 93.15 / 106.85. Target: realized vol > 30% sustained; Stop: 21 DTE with no realized payoff; Exit: target, stop, or vega goal hit.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Delta-neutral needs a cheap, liquid hedge instrument or you bleed the edge on re-hedging. Screen the underlying: tight stock spread (penny-wide, e.g. mega-cap or index ETF), option OI > 2,000 at hedge strikes, bid/ask < 5% of mid, shares/futures freely shortable, no hard-to-borrow. Free tools: barchart.com (HV vs IV), optionstrat.com (free position-Greek builder to see net Δ live), Cboe data. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): SPY, QQQ (penny-wide stock + deep options = cheapest re-hedge), AAPL for single-name; AVOID thin names where hedge slippage swamps the gamma payoff.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: delta-neutral is a comforting label that hides the real risks. (1) Neutral lasts a moment — gamma, charm and vanna re-introduce delta constantly, so an un-tended book is NOT neutral by lunch. (2) Every re-hedge pays the spread + commission — a certain cost against an uncertain gamma payoff. (3) "Neutral" only zeroes delta; a vega or gamma shock (Aug 2024) still wrecks you.

Honest edge — falsifiable metric: log cumulative re-hedge cost (slippage + commissions) versus cumulative gamma/vega P&L. If hedge friction > the Greek payoff over a meaningful sample, you have construction, not edge. Verdict: Skill-dependent — it is plumbing, not alpha; the edge lives in the underlying vol bet and in hedging cheaply, not in "being neutral."

L3 · Advanced

Gamma Scalping Gamma剝頭皮

Own positive gamma, delta-hedge dynamically, and monetize the gap when REALIZED vol beats the IMPLIED vol you paid in theta — a clean idea that retail costs usually eat.

Skill-dependent → Crowded
Prereq: Delta-neutral · VRP / variance · execution & slippage

① 機制 Mechanism

Plain first: buy options to be long gamma (e.g. an ATM straddle), then keep the position delta-neutral by trading the underlying. Because you are long gamma, every move re-creates delta in the profitable direction — spot up → you've accumulated positive delta → sell stock high; spot down → you've gone negative delta → buy stock low. You are mechanically forced to "buy low, sell high" around your hedge band, banking small scalps. Those scalps are your gross profit; the theta you pay each day is the rent. The whole game is whether the path delivers enough movement (realized vol) to out-earn the theta (implied vol you bought). It is the operational, path-by-path expression of the VRP — taken from the LONG side.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

The static long-straddle payoff above is only the starting inventory — gamma scalping converts that V-shape into a path-dependent P&L. The governing identity (continuous-hedge limit): daily P&L ≈ ½·Γ·(ΔS)² − Θ·dt (plus a vega term if IV moves). Gross gamma capture grows with the square of each move; theta is the fixed daily drag. Breakeven is when realized variance over the hold equals the implied variance you paid — i.e. realized vol = the IV you bought. Above it you profit; below it the theta wins.

½·Γ·(ΔS)²gamma capture / move
− Θ·dtdaily theta rent
σ_real = σ_implbreakeven vol

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Δ ≈ 0 (re-hedged constantly), Γ > 0 (the asset — bigger near ATM and as expiry nears), Θ < 0 (the rent that exactly offsets gamma if realized = implied), V > 0 (a side exposure — IV expansion is a bonus, contraction a tax; you can hedge it and remain a pure realized-vol bet, per error #5). The signature tension is Γ vs Θ: short-dated options give the most gamma per dollar but the steepest theta, so near-expiry scalping needs a LOT of realized movement, fast, to pay. The vega term means a gamma scalp can win on path (realized) yet lose on a vol-crush, or vice-versa — keep the two attributions separate.

Gamma-scalp schematic. Long gamma re-hedging buys delta into every dip (teal) and sells it into every rip (gold), monetizing the underlying's wiggle as realized-vol P&L (≈ ½Γ·ΔS²). Against that you bleed theta (Θ·dt) every day. Net is positive only when realized vol exceeds the implied vol you paid — and after subtracting transaction costs and discrete-hedge slippage, which widen the breakeven gap.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: you expect FORWARD REALIZED vol > the IV you can buy, in a choppy/trending tape, with a cheap hedge. It shines when implied is depressed (low IVR) right before a real, non-binary pick-up in movement — and when the underlying actually moves around (oscillation feeds scalps) rather than drifting quietly. It FAILS when: (1) realized comes in below the IV you paid (theta wins — the common case, because the VRP says implied is usually too high); (2) the tape goes dead-quiet (no moves to scalp, full theta drag); (3) re-hedging costs (spread + commission + slippage + discrete-hedge error) eat the thin gamma capture — the retail killer.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

SPX / SPY, week of Aug 5, 2024 (as-of 2026-06-17). A long-gamma delta-hedged book going INTO that week was the rare regime where scalping pays spectacularly: VIX gapped to an intraday 65.73 on Aug 5 and SPX swung violently for days, so realized vol crushed the ~16–17 IV you could have bought the prior week (est.) — every re-hedge of the exploding delta banked a real scalp, and long vega added a windfall. The mirror lesson is the 99% case: in the calm months before Aug, the same long-gamma book bled theta daily because realized stayed below implied (the VRP working against the buyer). The episode also exposes discrete-hedge risk — in a one-way pre-market gap you cannot re-hedge continuously, so part of the textbook ½·Γ·(ΔS)² capture is lost to the size of the jump. Sources: CME, BIS Bulletin 95, Macroption (verified ≥2; intraday high not close; yen-carry a driver not sole cause).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook
