Worked trade ticket (illustrative, reconstructed est.) — Long-gamma scalp on a normalized $100 underlying, IVR ≈ 20. Buy 1× 45-DTE 100 straddle for ~$7.85 debit ($785), IV 28%. Initial Δ ≈ 0, Γ ≈ +0.04/share, Θ ≈ −$9/day, V ≈ +$14/vol pt (×100, est.). Re-hedge rule: trade shares to flatten delta each time net |Δ| > 0.20/share (≈ a ±$5 move; up ~$5 → sell ~20 sh; down ~$5 → buy ~20 sh). Max loss: the $785 debit (plus net hedge slippage) if it sits dead at 100 to expiry. BPR ≈ debit. Breakeven vol ≈ 28% realized over the hold. Target: cumulative scalps + vega gain > theta paid, bank at +30% of debit; Stop: realized clearly < 28% by 21 DTE → close; Exit: target, stop, or vol-crush.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

You need MAX realized-vol-per-theta and MIN hedge cost. Screen: underlying with penny-wide stock spread (so re-hedging is nearly free), option OI > 3,000 ATM, option bid/ask < 4% of mid, current IV in the bottom third of its 1-yr range (IVR < 30), and a credible reason realized will pick up (NOT a binary earnings — that is a vol-crush trap). Free tools: barchart.com (IV vs HV ratio < 1 = implied below realized), marketchameleon.com (IV/HV screener, free tier), optionstrat.com (live Greeks). Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): SPY / QQQ (cheapest hedge, deepest chains) when index IVR is low; single names only if penny-wide and IV is genuinely depressed pre-move.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha that blows it up for retail: the ½·Γ·(ΔS)² identity is the frictionless, continuous-hedging ideal. In reality you hedge discretely (band breaches, not every tick) — that discrete-hedge error can flip a winning path negative, and every re-hedge pays the spread, commission and slippage. Against a VRP that says implied is usually rich, the long-gamma buyer starts behind, and the costs are the difference between the textbook and the brokerage statement.

Honest edge — falsifiable metric: log cumulative gamma scalps MINUS (theta paid + total re-hedge friction). If that net is positive across a real sample on YOUR fills, edge exists. Verdict: Skill-dependent → Crowded-low-edge for retail — the math is sound and pros run it near continuously with near-zero costs, but retail commissions, spreads and discrete hedging usually convert the textbook edge into a slow bleed unless you have a genuine realized->implied view and a penny-wide hedge.

L3 · Advanced

Short-Vol / VRP Harvesting 賣波動率（變異數風險溢酬收割）

Systematically sell options so you collect the premium that implied vol trades above what realized vol turns out to be — paid for carrying short-gamma (realized-move) risk, not for being short vega.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Variance vs vol & VRP · Delta-neutral · Gamma scalping · Risk & sizing

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: implied volatility — the price the option market charges for future movement — has historically sat above the volatility the index actually delivers. That persistent gap is the variance risk premium (VRP): the SPX vol seller is, on average, paid for insuring others against moves. You harvest it by repeatedly selling options (short straddles/strangles, put spreads, condors, or a short variance/vol position) and collecting the decay.

The correction that matters most here [correction #5]: VRP is compensation for short GAMMA — the risk that realized vol over your holding period blows past what you sold — not a reward for being short vega. Vega (sensitivity to the level of implied vol) is a separate exposure you can hedge: a short ATM straddle delta-hedged daily can be run near vega-neutral while still short gamma, isolating the realized-vs-implied bet. Conversely you can be long vega (own a calendar) yet short gamma. Do not fuse "theta/vega harvesting" into one idea — theta you collect is the flip side of the gamma you are short. The realized-vol identity (here Γ is your position gamma, which is negative for a short straddle): position P&L ≈ ½·Γ·(realized² − implied²)·S²·dt — with Γ < 0, the P&L is positive when realized < implied and negative when realized > implied. In words: you earn when realized < implied; you bleed when realized > implied.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Canonical expression = short ATM straddle (sell call + sell put, same strike/expiry). Tent-shaped payoff: max gain at the strike, losses widen linearly past breakevens and are theoretically unbounded on the upside / large on the downside.

≈ net creditmax profit (spot pins strike)
unbounded ↑ / large ↓max loss (undefined-risk)
K ± creditbreakevens · POP ≈ 60–70% for ~1σ wings (est.)

The shape is the whole story of why it kills accounts: a negatively skewed P&L — many small wins, rare enormous losses. The defined-risk cousin (short put spread / iron condor) trades premium for a capped tail and is the only version a retail book should run undefined-free.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Short straddle at initiation: Δ ≈ 0 (set neutral), Γ < 0 (short gamma — the risk you are paid for; gets violently negative as spot approaches the strike near expiry), Θ > 0 (positive decay — your daily income), V < 0 (short vega — you lose if IV rises, separate from realized). Magnitudes: gamma and theta are largest near the strike and accelerate into the last weeks; the gamma/theta tradeoff is exact — high positive theta is the rent you collect for carrying negative gamma.

Key nuance: you can hedge V toward zero (e.g., own further-dated vega or a long VIX-call sliver) and still keep Γ < 0 — that is what "harvesting VRP without a vega view" looks like. The gamma cannot be hedged away without buying back the option you sold (that is the whole position).

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: view = range-bound / mean-reverting · IV regime = elevated IVR where IMPLIED is rich relative to your forward-realized estimate · event = none inside the holding window. The edge is NOT "IVR is high" — it is "implied vol is priced above the realized vol that will actually unfold." High IVR with a coming macro shock can mean implied is cheap. Works in calm-after-fear regimes (IV elevated, tape settling). Fails catastrophically when realized vol expands faster than the credit can cushion — gap moves, regime breaks, vol-of-vol spikes — because short gamma convexity works against you precisely when it is biggest.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Volmageddon — 5 Feb 2018 (XIV / SVXY). A crowded short-vol complex met a single violent realized-vol day. VIX opened near 18 and closed near 37 — roughly a +100% one-day jump (largest one-day VIX point move at the time). The VelocityShares inverse-VIX ETN XIV — about $1.9B AUM — saw its indicative value collapse ~96% in the 4:00–4:15pm after-hours window; Credit Suisse issued an irrevocable call notice and terminated it 6 Feb 2018. ProShares cut SVXY leverage from −1.0x to −0.5x (eff. 28 Feb 2018) and UVXY from 2x to 1.5x. (Sources: SEC 8-K filings, Cboe, sixfigureinvesting, CFA Institute FAJ.) The reflexive mechanism — short-vol ETPs forced to buy VIX futures into the spike to stay market-neutral, amplifying the move — is well-documented; the exact dealer/ETP rebalance MAGNITUDE is inferred (est.). Lesson: short gamma at leverage is fine until the one day it is not, and that day pays for all the small wins many times over.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen for liquid, mean-reverting, NON-binary underlyings: option OI > 5,000 on target strikes; bid/ask < 5% of mid (penny-wide on SPY/QQQ/IWM); IVR > 50; NO earnings/FOMC/major macro inside DTE (binaries make realized vol un-forecastable); avoid single-name takeover/litigation risk. Free screener: Barchart "IV Rank/Percentile" + unusual-options screeners; tastytrade/tastylive liquidity rankings; Cboe for index data. As-of 2026-06-17 candidate underlyings (est., verify live): index proxies SPY / QQQ / IWM (deepest, definable-risk versions), and high-IVR liquid large-caps when IVR > 50 with no near earnings.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: the P&L is negatively skewed and short-gamma convexity is largest exactly when you are most wrong. Add leverage (ETPs, undersized BPR, or "it always comes back" averaging) and a single realized-vol day deletes a year of credits — the Volmageddon shape. Vega bleed (IV rising) hurts mark-to-market even when spot is fine, and forced-liquidation / margin-call mechanics (F6) can take the seat from you at the worst tick.

Honest edge — The falsifiable metric: log (implied vol sold) − (realized vol over the actual hold) per trade and your slippage vs mid; edge exists only if that spread stays net-positive AFTER costs and AFTER the rare tail losses (which small samples understate). VRP is real and persistent, but discrete-hedge error, bid/ask, and the negatively-skewed tail eat most retail attempts. Verdict: Skill-dependent — genuine premium, but only with disciplined sizing, defined-risk wings, and ruthless cut-rules; otherwise Crowded-low-edge after costs.

L3 · Advanced

Vol Term Structure 波動率期限結構

Two different curves wear the word "term structure" — the VIX-FUTURES curve you roll (and that ETPs ride) versus the SPX-OPTIONS implied-vol curve a calendar actually trades — and conflating them is how people mis-trade vol.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: IV & surface · Calendars · VRP · Data sources

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down, the single most important distinction [correction #8]:

(A) VIX-FUTURES term structure = the curve of VX futures prices across expiry months. This is what an ETP path / VX roll trade rides. In calm markets it is in contango (far months > near months > VIX spot), so a long-vol ETP (VXX/UVXY) holding front-month futures bleeds as each contract "rolls down" to spot at expiry — that roll yield / path dependence is why VXX is a long-run melt-down and why inverse ETPs (SVXY) earned the contango carry — until they blew up (L3-4). In stress the curve inverts to backwardation (near > far) and the bleed flips to a tailwind.

(B) SPX-OPTIONS implied-vol term structure = the curve of option-implied vols across expiries for SPX itself (e.g., 7d vs 30d vs 90d IV). In calm it slopes UPWARD (longer-dated IV > short-dated). This is what a calendar/diagonal trades (L2-6): on an upward curve the near is the cheaper IV but it decays faster, so a long calendar SELLS the near for its faster theta and BUYS the far for its vega — it is a theta-differential + long-vega trade (collect the differential decay if spot pins; profit if the back-month IV rises or the curve steepens). It is not "sell rich near / buy cheaper far" on this curve — that "sell the rich front" framing only applies when the front is flat or inverted (e.g. a pre-event front IV bid above the back; see facet ④). It is NOT the futures curve — though they are cousins, they price different things and can move differently.

VIX spot is not tradable. You cannot buy "the VIX" — only VX futures, VIX options (settled on the SOQ), or ETPs that hold those. So every "the VIX is low/high" idea must be expressed through one of those instruments, each with its own carry.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

This is a pure-concept / multi-instrument module — no single-expiry option engine applies. The two curves and their carry are drawn by hand:

Two distinct term structures. LEFT (VIX futures): the VX curve is normally in contango (far &gt; near), so rolling long-VIX-ETP exposure bleeds negative carry; the dashed red inset shows the stress regime flipping to backwardation (near &gt; far). RIGHT (SPX-options IV): the implied-vol curve slopes upward, near-dated IV is LOWER than far-dated. A long calendar therefore SELLS the near leg — the cheaper IV, but with faster theta decay — and BUYS the far leg for its richer vega; its edge is the theta differential, which only pays off if spot pins the strike into the near expiry. Note the inverse event case: when the FRONT is elevated/backwardated around a catalyst, you instead sell the rich front and buy the cheaper back for an IV-crush calendar.

P&L drivers: for a VX-roll / ETP trade, the edge is roll yield = (front future − VIX spot) accruing each day in contango, against the tail risk of a backwardation spike (the SVXY/XIV failure). For a SPX IV-term-structure (calendar) trade on the normal upward curve, the edge is the theta differential (the near you sold decays faster than the far you own) plus long vega (the back-month IV rising or the curve steepening), and the killer is a large spot move before front expiry (see L2-6 — calendars are long-vega, short-gamma, so the worst enemy is a big spot move, not time). Breakevens are path/IV-dependent before front expiry, not a clean expiry line.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

A long-the-back / short-the-front SPX calendar expressing an IV-term-structure view is long vega (net), short gamma, positive theta until the front expires — its enemy is a big spot move, not time. A VX-futures roll position has no option Greeks per se; its analogues are "long/short forward vol" with roll carry as the dominant P&L term and vol-of-vol as the convexity risk. The metric that governs how violently the futures curve itself can whip is VVIX (the "VIX of VIX," from Cboe, 2012) — VVIX is typically higher and more volatile than VIX and stays loosely correlated except in stress, when both jump together. High VVIX = the term-structure trade can re-price fast.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: VX-roll carry works in persistent calm with steep contango (collect roll), and fails the instant the curve inverts in a vol spike. SPX IV-term-structure (calendar) has two regimes: (i) the standard long calendar on the normal upward curve (front IV ≤ back) works by harvesting the theta differential + back-month vega when spot sits still through front expiry; (ii) the event variant works only when the front is rich vs the back (a flat/inverted front, e.g. pre-event where short-dated IV is bid) AND you expect the post-event move to be small — there you genuinely "sell the rich front." Both fail on a large spot move or on the back-month IV collapsing. Critical: a steep VIX-futures contango does NOT automatically mean SPX option calendars are attractive — check the actual SPX IV term structure, because the two curves are not interchangeable.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

5 Aug 2024 — the term structure inverts violently. After the BoJ hiked +15bp on 31 Jul 2024 and the yen-carry unwind hit, VIX went from a prior close near 23 to a pre-open / indicative intraday high near 65 (its largest one-day spike) before closing near 38. Note carefully: 65 was an intraday/indicative print during a thin SPX-options open, NOT a close — and Cboe confirmed the index priced per methodology (it was not "broken"). The yen carry was a driver, not the sole cause. The term-structure lesson: the VIX-futures curve slammed from contango into deep backwardation in hours — every contango-carry / short-vol roll trade took its tail loss at once, and any SPX calendar long the back/short the front got run over by the spot move. As-of magnitude on the indicative high is est. (intraday, illiquid open).

Sources: Cboe tail-event attribution (VIX methodology / 5 Aug 2024); IBKR Traders' Insight, Aug 2024.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked trade ticket — SPX IV-term-structure (standard long calendar on the upward curve), illustrative reconstructed est. ⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

For SPX IV-term-structure trades use SPX / SPY / QQQ only (deepest, penny-wide, no single-name binaries): OI > 10,000, bid/ask < 3% of mid, an upward-sloping curve (back-minus-front IV > 3 vol pts → sell the faster-decaying near, own the back vega), no FOMC/CPI/major macro between the two expiries. For VX-roll exposure, read the curve directly. Free sources: vixcentral.com (live VX futures term structure & contango/backwardation), Cboe VIX/VIX1D/VVIX data, tastylive / Barchart for SPX IV by expiry. As-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify live): watch SPY/QQQ calendar candidates when the SPX IV curve is steeply upward-sloping with no macro event inside the window.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: conflating the two curves. A steep VIX-futures contango is NOT a green light for SPX option calendars — they price different surfaces and can diverge. And VIX-spot is untradable, so every idea inherits the carry/path-dependence of the actual instrument (VXX bleed, ETP rebalance risk, SOQ settlement quirks on VIX options). VVIX rising warns the whole term structure can whip before you can adjust.

Honest edge — The falsifiable metric: for VX-roll, log roll yield captured vs the size of your worst backwardation drawdown — edge survives only if carry > tail over a full cycle (it usually does NOT survive an unhedged Volmageddon/Aug-2024). For SPX calendars, log realized theta-differential + back-month vega captured vs realized spot move (and, for the event variant only, realized front-IV convergence). Verdict: Skill-dependent — the structure (contango carry, mean-reverting term premium) is real, but path-dependence and rare inversions make it Pro-adjacent; defined-risk-only for retail.

L3 · Advanced

Skew & Smile Trading 偏斜與微笑

Trade the SHAPE of the vol surface across strikes — buying or selling the persistent equity put-skew via risk reversals — rather than the level of vol or direction.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: IV & surface · Parity & synthetics · VRP

① 機制 Mechanism

Plot implied vol against strike for one expiry and equity indexes don't show a flat line — they show skew: out-of-the-money puts trade at materially higher IV than OTM calls (a downward-sloping curve), with single names often closer to a symmetric smile. The standard measure is the 25-delta risk reversal = IV(25Δ call) − IV(25Δ put); for indexes it is persistently negative (puts richer). You trade the shape with a risk reversal: sell the rich OTM put + buy the cheaper OTM call (long the skew flattening / long the underlying via skew), or the reverse. Because, by put-call parity (F3), a long-call/short-put at the same strike is synthetic long stock, a risk reversal is "directional exposure financed by selling expensive skew" — you must separate the skew bet from the delta it carries.

Why skew exists: (1) Crash-o-phobia — after the 1987 crash the market began pricing fat left-tails; index puts carry a permanent crash premium. (2) Supply/demand — structural, price-insensitive demand for downside hedges (portfolio insurance, overwriters' collars) bids puts; covered-call supply caps OTM-call IV. (3) Leverage/vol correlation — equity vol rises as price falls, so the model itself implies higher put IV.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

The vol surface itself is a concept-SVG (the engine renders payoff, not the IV-by-strike curve):

Equity options carry a downward-sloping implied-vol skew: out-of-the-money puts trade at higher IV than calls because investors persistently bid downside protection (crash-o-phobia). The two overlays show what happens when spot moves: sticky-strike (blue) keeps IV pinned to each strike so the new ATM slides down the existing curve, while sticky-delta / sticky-moneyness (purple) drags the whole curve along with spot, holding ATM IV constant. Which regime dominates drives realized hedge P&L and the sign of vanna/volga exposure.

A short-put + long-call risk reversal (both OTM, same expiry — here short the 95 put, long the 108 call) is a synthetic-long-stock-like, +delta, short-skew position: between the two strikes the payoff is FLAT (no stock owned, neither leg in-the-money), then it tilts long — gains run unbounded above the long call and losses run large below the short put. The breakevens sit RELATIVE TO the strikes shifted by the net premium — NOT inside the flat zone:

Net-CREDIT RR (you collect credit C = put IV richer than call): between Kp and Kc the P&L is flat and positive = +C. There is only ONE breakeven, on the DOWNSIDE, where the assigned put eats the credit: S = Kp − C. Above Kc the gain is unbounded, so there is no upside breakeven. With short 95P / long 108C for a net credit of $0.80: flat P&L of +$0.80 ($80/contract) for any spot between 95 and 108; sole breakeven at S = 95 − 0.80 = $94.20; below it you are assigned the put and lose (Kp − S) − C.

Net-DEBIT RR (you pay debit D = call costs more than put fetches): the flat zone between the strikes is a small loss = −D. The breakeven is on the UPSIDE, where the long call recovers the debit: S = Kc + D. Below Kp the P/L is S − Kp − D — already a loss at Kp (−D) and worsening as the short put goes ITM, so there is no downside breakeven; the only breakeven is the upside one at S = Kc + D.

The engine renders the net-credit case below (explicit per-share net credit $0.80 wired into the ticket so the chart matches): flat at +$0.80 between 95 and 108, unbounded above 108, large loss below 95 with the single breakeven at $94.20:

unbounded ↑ (long call)max profit (rally above 108)
large ↓ ≈ −$9,420 (short 95 put to 0)max loss (selloff)
S = 94.20 (downside only)single breakeven = Kp − C; none on the upside

Pure skew trades (sell put-spread vs buy call-spread, or trade the 25Δ RR delta-hedged) strip out the delta to isolate the put-vs-call IV differential.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

A short-put / long-call risk reversal is net long delta (synthetic long, unless delta-hedged), typically near flat vega if sized to offset (long-call vega vs short-put vega), and long the call wing / short the put wing in higher-order Greeks (long the right tail, short the left tail). The two regime models that determine how skew re-prices as spot moves:

Sticky-strike: each strike keeps its IV as spot moves (so ATM IV changes as you slide along a fixed curve) — common in calm, range-bound tapes. Sticky-delta (sticky-moneyness): the IV-by-delta curve stays put, so the whole smile shifts with spot — common in trending/stressed tapes. Which regime holds changes your effective delta (the "vanna" effect: vol moving with spot), so a skew trade's realized delta can differ sharply from the static Black-Scholes delta. Knowing the regime is the skill.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: view = skew is mispriced relative to your forward-realized-skew estimate · IV regime = put-skew unusually steep (rich) or unusually flat (cheap) by its own history · event = you have a thesis on whether the next regime is sticky-strike or sticky-delta. Selling steep put-skew (short-skew) works in calm, mean-reverting tapes where the crash premium overpays; it fails in a crash, where realized skew explodes and the short put wing is exactly the tail that detonates (same negatively-skewed shape as L3-4). Buying cheap skew (long puts/long the RR's downside) works ahead of a regime break. Edge requires comparing implied skew to realized skew, not just eyeballing the curve.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

The structural birth of equity skew — pre vs post Oct-1987. Before the 1987 crash, US equity options showed little skew; afterward the persistent downward put-skew appeared and never left, as the market repriced fat left-tails (the documented "crash-o-phobia" shift — Wikipedia/Volatility-smile, Rubinstein's work). The durable lesson for a skew trader: the put premium is a structural insurance bid, not a temporary inefficiency, so "skew looks expensive, sell it" is the trade that works for years and then loses everything in one regime break (cf. 5-Aug-2024 and Feb-2018, where realized skew/vol blew past implied). The level/steepness numbers vary by date; the existence and persistence of index put-skew is the verified, load-bearing fact (≥2 sources: Wikipedia Volatility-smile, Cboe SKEW index docs). Exact historical 25Δ-RR levels are date-specific (mark est. if reconstructed).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
Entry gate25Δ RR > ~1 std-dev from its 1yr mean (skew rich or cheap by its own history)
Delta / strike25Δ put vs 25Δ call legs; delta-hedge to isolate skew, or accept the synthetic-long delta deliberately
DTE window30–60 DTE (skew most stable / liquid)
Profit targetRR reverts ~50% toward its mean
Stop / time-stopRR moves another 1 std-dev against; close at 21 DTE

Defend triggerActionResulting structureNew risk
Spot drops, put wing tested, regime turning sticky-deltaBuy back / roll down the short put; cut deltaReduced short-skew / flatter RRLocks loss on the put wing

Worked trade ticket — Short-skew risk reversal (illustrative, reconstructed est.). Underlying SPY $540; 25Δ RR at −7 vol pts (puts rich, >1σ above mean). Sell 45-DTE 515P (25Δ), buy 565C (25Δ), 1 RR, delta-hedge with ~−30 shares to isolate skew. Net credit ≈ $1.10 ($110). Max loss large on a selloff (short put wing) — model −3σ ≈ −$2,900; est. BPR ≈ $8,000. Target: RR reverts to −4 (close ~50% of move). Stop: RR to −10 OR put wing breached. Exit: target, stop, or 21 DTE.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Skew trades need a liquid, well-defined surface: SPY/QQQ/IWM and the deepest large-caps. OI > 5,000 on the 25Δ wings; bid/ask < 5% of mid; 25Δ RR sitting > 1 std-dev from its trailing mean; no earnings/macro binary inside DTE (single-name earnings warps skew unpredictably). Free sources: Cboe SKEW index + tastylive/Barchart IV-by-strike; track 25Δ RR over time on a charting platform. As-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify live): index ETFs (SPY/QQQ) for stable index put-skew; large-cap single names only when their RR is statistically stretched and earnings are clear of the window.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: "index put-skew is always expensive, so sell it" is right until a crash, when realized skew/vol explodes and the short put wing — the very thing you sold cheap insurance on — becomes the tail that eats years of credits (the L3-4 negative-skew shape again). And the sticky-strike vs sticky-delta regime determines your real delta: a static Black-Scholes hedge silently leaves you long/short as the smile shifts (vanna risk). Skew is a structural insurance premium, not a free inefficiency.

Honest edge — The falsifiable metric: log implied 25Δ skew at entry vs realized skew over the hold (and your realized vs static delta to catch the regime). Edge exists only if implied skew systematically overstates realized skew net of costs AND you survive the regime breaks. Verdict: Skill-dependent — genuine, persistent skew premium, but it is a tail-selling trade in disguise; sticky-strike/delta regime calls and disciplined tail-sizing separate edge from a delayed blow-up. Crowded-low-edge for naive short-skew.

L3 · Advanced

Earnings Vol-Crush 財報波動率壓縮（事件型隱波塌陷）

A binary-event trade where implied vol ramps INTO the print then collapses the instant uncertainty resolves — you are betting realized move vs the priced-in expected move, not direction.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: IV & surface · Mechanics & pin · Calendars · Straddles/Strangles

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: an earnings date is a scheduled variance bomb. Demand to hedge/bet the outcome bids up option premium, so front-cycle implied vol (IV) inflates for days before the print and is highest at the close right before it. The instant results drop, the unknown becomes known — IV on that cycle collapses overnight (the "crush") regardless of which way the stock went. The market's own forecast of the one-day move is readable directly off the chain. One convention, used everywhere below: the earnings expected move (EM) ≈ the front-expiry ATM straddle price — this is the common desk proxy, and we place break-even wings at strike ± EM. (Theoretical benchmark only: the continuous-time 1σ move S × IV × √(DTE/365) ≈ straddle × 1.25 is larger and differs because the front straddle embeds the discrete event jump rather than a smooth diffusion — do not size off it; size off the straddle.) So the cleanest framing: long premium (straddle/strangle) bets realized move > EM; short premium (iron condor / calendar) bets realized move < EM AND harvests the crush. The crush is the only part that is near-certain; the move is not.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Two opposite event structures. Long strangle (buy the move, accept the crush) — long Δ-neutral, +Γ, +V, −Θ; wins only on a move bigger than the EM you paid for. With spot $100 and EM ≈ ±$7, the break-even wings sit at strike ± EM, i.e. 93P / 107C:

Iron condor (sell the move, harvest the crush) — Δ-neutral, −Γ, −V, +Θ; wins if the stock stays inside the wings AND IV deflates. Short strikes at strike ± EM (93/107), protective wings $5 beyond, for the $1.40 net credit of the §⑥ ticket:

EM ≈ ATM straddleearnings expected move; wings at strike ± EM
~48 ppNVDA IV swing around a print (est., Q1-FY25)
70–75%of names stay inside EM

The calendar variant (sell the front high-IV expiry, buy a back lower-IV expiry at the same strike) is a third route — but it is multi-expiry and long-vega / short-gamma, so its P&L is path/IV-dependent, NOT a clean expiry line, and a LARGE post-print gap is its enemy (the short gamma): ATM implied vol gets steadily bid up in the days into an earnings print as traders pay for event uncertainty, peaking just before the after-close release, then collapses ("IV crush") the next morning once the result is known — long options bought purely for the event lose value even if direction is right. The teal expected-move band is the price range implied by the ATM straddle; the stock's realized move most often lands inside it, which is why short-premium structures win more often than they lose around earnings.

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

The trade is dominated by vega, not delta. Long strangle: ~0 net delta, long vega (hurt by the crush), long gamma (helped by a big gap), heavily negative theta into the print. Iron condor: ~0 net delta, short vega (the crush is your friend — this is the whole edge), short gamma (a gap past a wing is the enemy), positive theta. Calendar: long vega on net but the asymmetry is the point — you are short the front cycle's fat IV (crushes hard) and long the back cycle's lower IV (crushes little), so a calendar mainly monetizes the term-structure differential in the crush, and is short gamma — it hates a large gap. Across all three, the post-print move in IV swamps theta and often swamps delta. The plot below is the iron condor (single-expiry, so the engine renders it cleanly):

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell for the short-premium version: a name whose options market habitually OVER-prices its earnings move (the front-cycle IV is rich vs the stock's historical post-earnings realized move) AND you have NO directional edge. The crush is the compensation for selling that rich event vol. It FAILS catastrophically on the names that gap further than EM — exactly the high-momentum AI/growth names retail loves. The long-premium version's cell is the mirror: you have a real reason to expect a move bigger than the (here, cheap) implied — but note that on most liquid names IV is rich into earnings, so long straddles start the race behind. Critical: IVR being high is NOT the signal — IV is ALWAYS high into a print. The signal is IV high relative to this name's own typical realized earnings move (the "implied-vs-realized earnings edge"), which you measure from a history table, not from IVR.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

NVDA Q1-FY25, reported after the close on May 22 2024 (as-of verification 2026-06-17). Heading in, the options market priced an EM (front ATM straddle) of roughly ~8%; the stock actually rose ~+9.3% the next session (May 23 2024 close ≈ $1,038 vs ≈ $950 prior; est., reconstructed from Barchart / Market Chameleon implied-vs-actual series). Front-cycle IV swung an estimated ~48 percentage points around the print (≈+15pp into it, ≈−33pp after) for that quarter (est., Market Chameleon). The lesson, both ways: the short-premium crowd that sold an iron condor inside ±8% got run over because realized > EM — the crush was real but the gamma loss was bigger. The long-strangle buyer who paid the rich ~8% straddle actually WON for once, because NVDA was the rare name then beating its EM (a ~9.3% realized vs ~8% EM is a positive but THIN margin — the buyer's net edge after the crush was modest, not the windfall the headline gap implies). Subsequent NVDA cycles repriced the straddle up toward ~10–11% (est.) — proof that "the crush is reliable" and "selling it is profitable" are different claims. Selling event vol only pays when implied has been consistently > realized; for momentum AI names in 2024–25 it frequently was NOT.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked trade ticket — Underlying: liquid large-cap reporting tomorrow, spot $100, front-cycle IVR 78, ATM straddle $7.00 → earnings expected move EM ≈ ±$7.00 (the front ATM straddle). Structure: short iron condor with shorts at strike ± EM — sell 93P / buy 88P, sell 107C / buy 112C (short strikes at ±$7 = the EM; protective wings $5 beyond), 2 DTE, 3 contracts. Net credit $1.40/share = $420 total. Max loss ($5 width − $1.40) × 100 × 3 = $1,080. BPR ≈ $1,080 (defined-risk). Breakevens $91.60 / $108.40 (short strike ± net credit). Target: buy back at $0.70 (50%) the morning after the print. Stop/time-stop: close by next open regardless. Roll trigger: gap through a wing → close tested side. Exit: crush realized, condor bought back for $0.65 at the open → +$225. (illustrative; premia reconstructed est.)

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Runnable screen: (1) earnings confirmed within 1–2 sessions; (2) front-cycle IVR >50; (3) the real gate — pull the last 8–12 earnings: median IMPLIED move > median REALIZED move by ≥1.5pp for short-premium (the over-pricing edge), or the reverse for long; (4) option OI >1,000 at the strikes, bid/ask <10% of mid on the wings; (5) weekly expiry available the week of the print. Free tools: Market Chameleon (free earnings implied-vs-realized history table — the key data), Barchart "Expected Move", OptionStrat / Option Profit Calculator for the payoff. Candidate watch as-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify the date and the implied-vs-realized table before any trade): the mega-cap AI names (NVDA, AVGO) tend to UNDER-price (favor long); slower mega-caps and consumer staples more often OVER-price (favor short-premium condors). Do not trade the AI momentum names short-vol into a print.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: the crush is reliable, profiting from selling it is NOT. Beginners see IV always falling after earnings and conclude short premium is free money — then one gap past the wing erases a dozen winners because the payoff is negatively skewed (small wins, occasional max-loss). On momentum names the realized move repeatedly exceeds the EM, so the "rich" vol was actually cheap. Second gotcha: pin/assignment if you carelessly hold a short condor into its own expiry (see F4).

Honest edge — Metric: over your last N≥20 earnings trades on a name-set, is mean (EM − realized move) reliably positive AFTER slippage and the occasional max-loss? If yes → genuine but thin and capacity-limited. For the typical retail trader selling event vol on the names they actually follow (high-momentum tech), the gap is usually negative. Verdict: Skill-dependent — real only if you systematically harvest names whose implied has been > realized and you never hold past the open; otherwise crowded-low-edge.

L3 · Advanced

Second-Order Greeks 二階希臘字母（Vanna · Charm · Vomma · Speed）

The curvature Greeks — how your delta and vega themselves move as vol, time, and spot drift — which is the entire mechanical engine behind dealer-flow regimes (L4-2 only applies what this module owns).

Skill-dependent
Prereq: The Greeks (full) · IV & surface

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: first-order Greeks (Δ, Γ, Θ, V) tell you how option value moves when ONE input nudges. Second-order Greeks tell you how those Greeks themselves move — they are the curvature, the "Greeks of the Greeks." Four matter:

Vanna = ∂Δ/∂σ = ∂Vega/∂S. How your delta changes when implied vol changes (equivalently, how vega changes when spot moves). A pure vol move — spot unchanged — silently shifts your delta. This is why a vol spike forces re-hedging even when the stock hasn't moved. Its sign is moneyness-, time-, and convention-dependent (worked out in facet ③), so "vanna is positive" is never true in the abstract. Charm = ∂Δ/∂t. How delta drifts purely from the passage of time ("delta decay"). It runs every hour, relentlessly pulling OTM deltas toward 0 and ITM deltas toward ±1 as expiry nears — strongest near expiry and around the money. Vomma = ∂V/∂σ. How vega changes as vol changes — the convexity of your vol exposure; it makes long-vega positions GAIN vega as IV rises (good) and is why far-OTM "vol of vol" trades behave non-linearly. Speed = ∂Γ/∂S. How gamma changes as spot moves — matters most for near-expiry / pinned positions where gamma is exploding and your delta hedge goes stale fast.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Second-order Greeks are not a standalone payoff — they are corrections to the first-order P&L attribution. The fuller expansion of a position's P&L is: ΔP ≈ δ·dS + ½γ·dS² + θ·dt + V·dσ + vanna·dS·dσ + ½vomma·dσ² + charm·dS·dt + … The first row is what most retail traders track; the bolded cross-terms are what actually moves an open position on a day when BOTH spot and vol move (i.e. every interesting day). There is no "breakeven" — instead the practical KPI is: which cross-term is biggest for YOUR book right now.

vanna·dS·dσspot×vol cross-P&L
charm·dS·dttime-decay of delta
½·vomma·dσ²vol-convexity P&L

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Signs and where they bite — and every sign below is conditioned on moneyness, time, and sign convention, never absolute. Vanna (∂Δ/∂σ): the sign is moneyness-dependent, not a fixed "positive for a long call." For a long call, vanna is generally POSITIVE for OTM strikes (a vol rise lifts an out-of-the-money delta toward 0.5) and NEGATIVE for ITM strikes (a vol rise pulls a deep delta back down toward 0.5), crossing through ~zero a bit ABOVE the ATM forward (not exactly at-the-money), with magnitude fading to near-zero deep ITM and deep OTM where delta is pinned at 1 or 0. (Long put vanna is the mirror; and the BS textbook closed form, −e^(−qτ)·n(d₁)·d₂/σ, makes the crossing-point and the deep-strike fade explicit.)

Dealer-flow intuition — the signed story (must match L4-2 exactly). Take the dominant index regime: customers and hedgers BUY downside index puts (crash protection), so dealers are NET SHORT those puts. A short put has POSITIVE delta, so the dealer holds SHORT stock as the hedge. Now apply vanna: a put's (negative) delta becomes MORE negative as IV rises, so the dealer — who is SHORT that put — sees their delta become MORE POSITIVE (longer) as IV rises. To return to neutral a longer book must SELL, so:

  • IV RISES → dealer delta rises → dealer SELLS stock to re-neutralize. Because vol-up usually coincides with price-down, this ADDS selling pressure: a DOWNSIDE ACCELERANT.
  • IV FALLS (market calms) → dealer delta falls → dealer BUYS BACK stock → upward drift. This is the "vanna rally" / grind-up — it is the IV-DOWN / calm case, NOT the vol-spike case.

So the net is: IV-down (or time passing in a calm tape) ⇒ dealers BUY (supportive); IV-up ⇒ dealers SELL (accelerant). This is exactly why skew matters — it is the cross-strike vanna profile, signed, that drives the flow — and why a sign error here flips a whole macro narrative. (The sign is convention-dependent: vanna is POSITIVE for OTM long calls but NEGATIVE for ITM long calls, so the conclusion above holds only under the stated short-put inventory; flip the inventory and you flip the flow.) Charm (∂Δ/∂t): pulls delta toward its expiry endpoint; an OTM long call LOSES delta over time (charm pushes it toward 0), an ITM one GAINS delta (toward 1) — so charm's sign too is moneyness-dependent. For the same short-put book with spot ABOVE the put strikes, those short puts are OTM, so their delta decays toward 0 as time passes — the dealer's hedge SHORT shrinks and the dealer BUYS BACK stock into expiry and over weekends = CHARM support, the calm-market pre-OPEX / Friday-afternoon drift up. Peaks in the final days/hours and around the money. Vomma (∂V/∂σ): positive for a plain long option and largest for OTM (away-from-ATM) strikes, shrinking toward ~zero for ATM options where vega is near its peak and flat in σ; gives long-vol positions their "wings" — vega grows as IV grows. Speed (∂Γ/∂S): gamma's own slope, sign flips around the strike (gamma rises into then falls past the peak); near expiry around the strike gamma is so peaked that speed makes hedges decay between ticks. Every one of these is a heuristic conditional on net dealer inventory and convention — they describe a mechanism, not a guaranteed flow. The visual below shows how vanna and charm flow re-hedging pressure: Vanna/charm dealer-flow for a dominant short-put book (dealers sold puts, hold short stock as hedge). Top arm (teal): IV down or time passing shrinks the short-put delta, so the short hedge is bought back, dealers BUY stock and the tape grinds up. Bottom arm (red): IV up (usually with price down) grows the put delta, dealers SELL more stock to re-hedge, accelerating the move down. Heuristic only, signs flip with net dealer inventory.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell where this knowledge pays: any position held across a day where spot AND vol move together, and especially large books near expiry — that is where first-order Greeks lie to you and the cross-terms dominate. Concretely: (a) explaining why your "delta-neutral" position made/lost money on a flat-spot, vol-down day (answer: vanna + vomma); (b) anticipating the predictable overnight / OPEX-week delta drift that charm imposes on the whole market (the engine L4-2 turns into the "charm/vanna grind-up in a calm, IV-falling tape into Friday" heuristic — supportive when vol falls and time passes, an accelerant only when vol spikes). It FAILS as a standalone trade — you do not "buy charm." It is a lens for attribution and for understanding dealer flow, not a payoff to own. Treating a second-order Greek as a tradeable signal on its own is the classic over-reach.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

The GME / meme-squeeze framing, Jan 2021 (verification as-of 2026-06-17). Price and volume are public and verifiable (GME ran from the low-$20s to an intraday ~$483 on Jan 28 2021, pre-split; later 4-for-1 split makes the per-share figure look lower on adjusted charts); short interest was extreme. This is the call-driven mirror of the index put regime in facet ③: a wall of OTM CALL buying forced dealers SHORT those calls (short call = negative delta → dealers buy stock to hedge). As spot rose and IV exploded, vanna (vol up → the long call's delta climbs toward 0.5 → the dealer's short-call delta goes MORE negative → dealer must BUY more stock) and charm (time passing on now deep-ITM calls → call delta toward 1 → short-call delta toward −1 → dealer must BUY more) mechanically forced dealer buying that amplified the move. Note the sign flip versus the index case: on a short-CALL book vol-up forces BUYING (squeeze fuel), whereas on the short-PUT index book of facet ③ vol-up forces SELLING — same vanna, opposite inventory, opposite flow. Honest caveat (graded): the price/volume/SI are facts, but the MAGNITUDE of dealer-inventory-driven hedging is an inference/estimate — no public dataset measures dealer gamma directly; it is reconstructed from OI and an assumed dealer-long/short model (cite SEC's GME staff report, OCC volume, exchange data). The lesson: second-order Greeks give the correct mechanism for why hedging flows can become reflexive, but the claimed size of that flow is always an estimate, never a measurement — the same humility L4-1's GEX requires.

Sources: SEC Staff Report "Equity and Options Market Structure Conditions in Early 2021" (Oct 14 2021, GME price/SI facts); OCC cleared-options volume data (as-of 2026-06-17).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

This is theory you APPLY to other modules, so the "playbook" is a measurement-and-attribution routine, not a strike-selection grid:

ParamDefault
When to computeAny book held overnight; ALWAYS in the final 5 DTE and OPEX week
Vanna watchFlag positions where a ±5 vol-pt move shifts net delta > 10% of account beta-weighted delta; remember vol-UP makes a short-put book LONGER (forces selling), vol-DOWN shorter (forces buying)
Charm watchEstimate the overnight delta drift on OTM shorts; pre-plan the hedge before the close, not at the open
Vomma watchOn long-vol books, expect vega to GROW into a vol spike — do not under-size the vega-loss tail

Flat spot, big vol drop, position lost money "for no reason"Attribute to vanna (delta shift) + vomma (vega convexity); re-hedge delta to true neutralRe-neutralized bookTransaction cost; over-hedging if vol mean-reverts

Worked attribution example — Hold a delta-neutral long 30-DTE straddle, spot flat at $100, but IV falls −4 vol-pts overnight. First-order story says "delta-neutral, spot flat → no P&L." Reality: vega ~$0.12/contract/vol-pt × −4 = −$48 per straddle (the V·dσ term), AND vomma made that vega itself shrink, AND any residual delta got nudged by vanna. The point of the ticket: the loss is fully explained by second-order terms — that is the skill, knowing WHICH Greek took your money. (illustrative est.)

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Not a ticker screen — a TOOL screen, since this is applied theory. You need a platform that surfaces second-order Greeks: (1) Thinkorswim Analyze tab can show vanna/charm/vomma per-position; (2) OptionNet Explorer / OptionStrat for second-order curves; (3) any free Black-Scholes calculator (e.g. the optionsmath.com / macroption sheets) to finite-difference them yourself. Verify the platform's sign convention before trusting it (vanna especially is signed inconsistently across vendors — the same "inference not measurement / check the sign" discipline as GEX in L4-1). Practice on the names you already trade with weeklies, so you can watch charm move delta in real time near expiry.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: treating a second-order Greek as a tradeable signal. You cannot "buy vanna" or trade charm directly; they are corrections to your P&L attribution and the mechanism behind dealer flow — not a strategy. The second trap is sign confusion: vendors define vanna/charm with different signs and you can mis-assume the dealer's net inventory, so a model that says "vol-up means dealers BUY" can be exactly backwards — on the dominant short-put index book, vol-up makes dealers LONGER and forces SELLING (accelerant), while it is vol-DOWN and time-passing-in-calm that make dealers BUY (the vanna rally / charm support). Get the inventory or the sign wrong and the whole regime call inverts. These Greeks explain the mechanism of reflexive hedging; they never tell you the magnitude, which is always inferred, and every flow conclusion is a heuristic conditional on net dealer inventory.

Honest edge — Metric: does adding second-order attribution measurably improve your hedging/timing P&L vs ignoring it (compare hedged-error before/after over N expiries)? For most retail books the answer is "marginally, only near expiry / OPEX." Verdict: Skill-dependent — genuinely useful as an attribution and dealer-flow lens (and a hard prerequisite for L4-2), but it is curvature awareness, not an edge you can monetize on its own.

L3 · Advanced

Ratio Spreads & Backspreads 比例價差與逆比例價差

Unbalanced-leg structures that intentionally engineer net long OR net short gamma/vega — a backspread buys convexity (more longs than shorts) for a tail move; a ratio spread sells it for income, at the price of an undefined-risk shoulder.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Verticals · The Greeks · Risk & sizing · Second-order Greeks

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: a vertical spread has matched legs (1×1). The moment you make the leg counts UNEQUAL, you create a directional gamma/vega bet by construction. Two mirror families. Call ratio backspread = sell 1 nearer call, buy 2 farther calls (1:2). Because you own MORE long options than you sold, the net position is long gamma, long vega, and — if the long calls are cheap enough — can be opened for a net credit, giving you a "free" tail bet: you profit big on a strong up-move, keep the credit if it collapses, and lose only in the middle. Call ratio spread (the "front spread") = buy 1 nearer call, sell 2 farther calls — the inverse: short gamma, short vega, collects premium, but the EXTRA naked short call gives it undefined upside risk. Puts mirror this for the downside. The whole module is about deliberately choosing your gamma sign and accepting the matching tail. Below, each structure gets its OWN payoff so the chart, the KPIs, and the ticket all describe the SAME position.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Call ratio / front spread (buy 1 lower call, sell 2 higher calls) — the leg array [{c,+1,100,prem 1.50},{c,−2,108,prem 0.95}] is 1 long lower call + 2 short higher calls. Skew makes the two short 108 calls (0.95 each = 1.90) richer than the one long 100 call (1.50), so the structure opens for a net CREDIT of 1.90 − 1.50 = +0.40/share (+$40): a small max profit pinned at the upper strike, then UNLIMITED loss above it (the naked-shoulder tail). Short gamma, short vega. The KPIs directly beneath describe THIS rendered figure:

+$840ratio max profit (pinned at upper 108 strike = 8 width + 0.40 credit)
Unlimitedratio max loss (runaway up-move above 116.40)
$116.40upper breakeven · keep the +$40 credit if it craters below 100

Call backspread (sell 1 lower call, buy 2 higher calls) — the exact inverse leg array [{c,−1,100,prem 2.30},{c,+2,108,prem 1.00}] = 1 short lower call + 2 long higher calls: a capped loss in the valley at the long strike, then convex UNLIMITED PROFIT on a big up-move. Here the one short 100 call (2.30) more than funds the two long 108 calls (1.00 each = 2.00), so this side opens for a small net CREDIT of 2.30 − 2.00 = +0.30/share (+$30) — the same credit carried into the worked ticket in ⑥. Long gamma, long vega. Same three strike levels as the ratio above — only the long/short counts flip, which flips the entire payoff. The KPIs beneath describe THIS rendered figure:

Unlimitedbackspread max profit (up-move above 115.70)
−$770backspread max loss (pinned at long 108 strike = 8 width − 0.30 credit)
$115.70upper breakeven · keep the +$30 credit if it craters below 100

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Backspread (more longs): net long gamma and long vega by construction — it WANTS a big move and a vol expansion; theta is a modest bleed in the valley; delta starts near-flat-to-slightly-directional and accelerates with spot (that is the long-gamma convexity). Ratio/front spread (more shorts): net short gamma, short vega, positive theta — it wants the stock to drift toward the long strike and sit there while IV falls, and it is endangered by exactly the tail the backspread loves. Because the leg counts are unequal, the delta is NOT static — it ramps as spot crosses the strikes (high speed, see L3-8). The first Greek curve is the ratio/front spread (the short-gamma side), so gamma reads NEGATIVE around the upper strike:

The second curve is the backspread (the long-gamma side, the mirror), so gamma reads POSITIVE around the upper strike — that positive gamma is the convexity you bought:

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

Call backspread's ONE cell: you expect a large up-move (a breakout / squeeze / event you think is under-priced) AND IV is LOW (you want to BUY vega cheap before it expands), AND you want defined cost — ideally a net credit so a no-show still pays. It FAILS in the dead zone: a small grind UP to the long strike at expiry is the worst case (max loss), and time decay in a flat tape bleeds it. Put backspread is the crash-convexity mirror — long downside gamma for a selloff, best when downside IV/skew is unusually cheap. Ratio (front) spread's cell: a mild directional drift TOWARD the long strike with FALLING IV and no tail risk in that direction — and you must be willing to wear undefined risk on the naked shoulder, which for retail is usually disqualifying. The single biggest mismatch retail makes: opening a backspread when IV is already HIGH (so you overpay for the long convexity and eat vega-crush) — the reverse of when it works.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Put backspread as crash-convexity, framed against Feb 5 2018 "Volmageddon" (verification as-of 2026-06-17). The verifiable facts: the S&P 500 fell ~−4.1% that day, VIX more than doubled intraday (one of its largest one-day jumps on record), and the short-vol ETP XIV saw its indicative value collapse roughly ~−96% after the close and was subsequently terminated by its issuer (Credit Suisse) — note many write-ups misstate this as a clean "−96% print" when it was the after-hours indicative value that triggered the acceleration event (cite Credit Suisse's termination notice / SEC). The lesson for THIS module: a trader long a put backspread (sell 1 near put, buy 2 far OTM puts) opened when downside IV was cheap would have seen the 2 long puts explode in a long-gamma, long-vega tail event — the structure is purpose-built to convert a fat-tailed crash into convex profit, and it can be opened near zero cost. The mirror warning: anyone SHORT that convexity (a put RATIO spread, i.e. the naked-shoulder side, or short-vol generally) was on the wrong end of the same gamma. The move size is fact; the dealer-flow amplification is an estimate.

Sources: Credit Suisse XIV acceleration/termination notice (Feb 2018); SEC/SIFMA post-mortem on the Feb 5 2018 volatility event; CBOE VIX historical data. As-of 2026-06-17.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Backspread: spot rips through long strikesRoll the short leg up/out to lock gains and reload convexityNew higher-strike backspreadPays a debit; resets the valley higher
Ratio spread: spot blowing through the naked shoulderBUY a further wing to cap it → convert to a vertical/butterflyDefined-risk fly or verticalLocks loss but removes the unlimited tail (do this BEFORE it runs)
Worked trade ticket (call ratio BACKSPREAD — the convex, defined-loss side; matches the second payoff/Greek figures above) — Underlying: low-IVR breakout candidate, spot $100, IVR 22. Sell 1 × 100C @ 2.30, buy 2 × 108C @ 1.00 each (2.00 total), 60 DTE. Net credit = 2.30 − 2.00 = $0.30/share = +$30 (the one short is richer than the two longs). Max loss = valley at 108 = (8 width − 0.30 credit) × 100 = $770. BPR ≈ that max loss (defined, since the extra long covers — broker margins it as the spread risk). Breakevens: lower side keep-the-credit below ~$100; upper breakeven ≈ $115.70 (above which profit is unlimited). Target: +$500 on a run to ~$122. Stop/time-stop: exit if spot sits $104–108 at 21 DTE (valley). Roll trigger: spot > $116 → roll short up. Exit: breakout to $123, close for +$510. (illustrative; premia reconstructed est.)

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Runnable screen for a call backspread: (1) IVR <30 (cheap convexity to buy); (2) a real up-catalyst inside the DTE window (breakout level, sector momentum) but NOT an earnings print between entry and expiry (the post-print crush kills the long vega); (3) liquid chain — OI >500 at all three strikes, bid/ask <8% of mid (you trade 3 legs, slippage compounds); (4) skew that lets the 2 longs be bought near the price of the 1 short (so it opens for credit/tiny debit). Free tools: Barchart or OptionStrat to screen low-IVR names and build the structure; Market Chameleon for the IVR/skew. Candidate archetypes as-of 2026-06-17 (est., verify IVR and earnings dates live): lower-IVR large-caps with a pending technical breakout suit a CALL backspread; for a PUT backspread, an index/ETF (SPY, QQQ) when downside skew/IV is unusually cheap is the cleaner crash-convexity vehicle. Avoid illiquid single names — the 3-leg slippage erases the credit edge.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha that blows it up: the ratio/front spread's naked shoulder is UNDEFINED risk — the extra short call/put can hand you an unlimited (calls) or large (puts) loss in exactly the tail you didn't expect, and assignment risk compounds it. Retail consistently under-prices this because the position "looks" like a small-credit income trade. For the backspread side the trap is opposite and subtler: opening it when IV is already HIGH (overpaying for convexity), and the valley — a slow grind to the long strike at expiry — is the silent max-loss zone people forget about. Either way, leg counts ≠ 1:1 means your delta and gamma RAMP, so "the position changed character on me" is the recurring complaint.

Honest edge — Metric: for the backspread, is realized IV/realized move at exit consistently above the IV you paid at entry (did you buy cheap convexity that paid)? For the ratio spread, does the harvested premium survive the occasional tail loss over N≥20 trades after slippage? Verdict: Skill-dependent — the backspread is a genuine, honest convexity buy when IVR is truly low and a catalyst is real (and is a cleaner retail tool than naked short vol); the income-side ratio spread is closer to crowded-low-edge / pro-only for retail because the undefined shoulder turns a string of small wins into one account-denting loss.

Use as regime context

GEX / dealer-gamma, vanna & charm, and 0DTE flow — to FILTER and time the trades you already have a thesis on.

Observe / paper only

Dispersion & correlation, box-spread financing — these need pro infrastructure; learn the mechanics, don't size them.

Avoid

Trading GEX or max-pain as a standalone signal; systematic retail short-premium 0DTE (pennies in front of a steamroller).

L4 · Professional

GEX / DEX — Dealer Gamma Positioning 伽瑪曝險（造市商部位推估）

An INFERRED map of how much hedging market-makers must do per move — read it as a coincident REGIME FILTER (calm-mean-reverting vs jumpy-trending), never as a free mechanical buy/sell signal.

Pro-only
Prereq: The Greeks · Second-order Greeks · Free data map

① 機制 Mechanism

Gamma Exposure (GEX) is an estimate of the net gamma that options dealers are assumed to hold across all strikes/expiries, expressed as the dollar amount of underlying they must trade to stay delta-neutral per 1% move. DEX is the analogous net-delta estimate — equally inferred, built on the same assumed dealer book. The standard model assumes dealers are long calls and short puts to customers — so the customer side flips the sign. Under that model: dealers long gamma hedge against the move (sell rallies, buy dips) which dampens realized vol; dealers short gamma hedge with the move (buy rallies, sell dips) which amplifies it. The gamma-flip / zero-gamma level is the spot where the modeled net dealer gamma crosses from positive to negative. Critical caveat: this is an INFERENCE built on three shaky inputs — (a) an assumed dealer-long-gamma starting position, (b) open interest that is stale (yesterday's close, not today's flow), and (c) a sign convention that differs between providers (SpotGamma vs SqueezeMetrics vs your own calc can disagree on direction). It is reverse-engineered, not observed.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

GEX has no payoff of its own — it is a context filter, not a position. The relevant "payoff" is what it does to the structures you already trade: in a positive-gamma (above flip) regime, range/premium-selling (condors, CSPs) face suppressed realized vol; in a negative-gamma (below flip) regime, the same short-premium books face fat-tailed gap risk while long-gamma/convex structures get tailwinds.

GEX profile: a gamma-by-strike bar chart centered on spot. Bars above the zero line are net positive dealer gamma (core strikes), bars below are negative (the wings); the white line marks spot and the gold dashed line the gamma-flip / zero-gamma level. Right-side legend contrasts the two regimes: in +gamma dealers sell rallies and buy dips (pinning / mean-reversion, suppressed vol), while in −gamma dealers chase price (trend-amplification, gap risk). This is an INFERENCE — dealer positioning is estimated from option open interest assuming dealers are short customer flow, so both the sign and the flip level can be wrong.
+GEXmodeled dealer-long → vol-dampening / mean-revert
−GEXmodeled dealer-short → vol-amplify / trend
flipzero-gamma pivot (an estimate, not a line in the sand)

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

GEX is aggregate position-gamma, re-aggregated to the dealer side. It tells you the second-order (gamma) hedging pressure, while DEX tells you the first-order (delta) supply/demand from hedging. Sign + magnitude both matter: a deeply negative GEX near a large negative-gamma strike wall means small spot moves force large same-direction dealer hedges — the structural recipe for an air-pocket. A large positive GEX concentrated near spot is the "pinning" setup — note this is gamma-driven pinning from modeled dealer hedging, a distinct concept from the max-pain heuristic (a dubious, crowded, post-hoc claim that price gravitates to the strike that maximizes aggregate option-holder loss), and distinct again from pin risk (the real assignment uncertainty you face at a short strike into expiry — see L4-4). The number drifts intraday as spot moves through strikes and as fresh flow (invisible to OI-based GEX until tomorrow) rewrites the book.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE honest use: a regime overlay on a thesis you already hold. Above flip with high +GEX → favor mean-reversion / premium-selling sizing; expect compression into known strike walls. Below flip with deep −GEX → respect trend, widen stops, cut short-premium size, prefer defined-risk and long-convexity. It fails when treated as a standalone signal: it is coincident-to-lagging (OI is stale), the dealer-long assumption breaks during dealer de-grossing, and crowding means the "obvious" flip level is front-run. It says nothing about direction, only about how the market is likely to move (smooth vs jumpy).

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

S&P 500, Aug 5 2024 (as-of episode). The VIX printed an intraday high of 65.73 but closed at 38.57 (prior close 23.39) — verified via Cboe/Macroption. Practitioner GEX trackers had flagged the tape as deeply negative-gamma below the flip going in, and the air-pocket-then-snapback price action is the textbook short-gamma fingerprint: dealers selling into weakness, then the spike collapsing as flow reset. But the magnitude of dealer short-gamma hedging that day is an inferred ESTIMATE, not a measured quantity — the move was also driven by a yen-carry unwind (BoJ +15bp on Jul 31; JPMorgan est. 65–75% of carry unwound by mid-August) and by illiquid pre-market SPX options distorting the VIX calc itself (VX futures stayed below 35 while spot VIX spiked — a reminder that the tradable instrument is VX futures, not the spot VIX index). Lesson: GEX correctly typed the regime (jumpy, trend-amplifying) but could not have sized or timed the move, and was one of several causes — exactly how to use it.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked usage ticket — Underlying SPX, as-of 2026-06-17 (illustrative). Pre-open read: aggregate GEX modeled negative, flip ~1.2% above spot, large −γ wall just below. Decision: existing 30-DTE iron condor (short 16Δ wings) — do NOT add the planned new condor; instead close the put credit spread for a $0.40 debit (locking partial gain) and keep only the call side. Net: book risk cut ~45%, no new short-premium added in a trend-amplifying regime. There is no "GEX trade" here — the edge is not being the seller into an air-pocket.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

GEX is only reliable on deeply-liquid, OI-rich underlyings: SPX/SPY, QQQ, top-5 mega-caps. Screen: index/ETF or single-name with options ADV >100k contracts AND aggregate OI >1M; AVOID thin single-names (OI too sparse for a meaningful aggregate). FREE sources: SqueezeMetrics publishes a daily aggregate S&P GEX; SpotGamma (free tier / educational charts) coined the metric; CBOE open-interest data + your own gamma aggregation for DIY. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): SPX/SPY (cleanest aggregate) and QQQ (clean, but tech-concentrated so single-name flow leaks in).

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The blow-up is false precision: treating an OI-derived, dealer-long-assumed, provider-sign-dependent estimate as if it were a measured order book, then sizing INTO a −GEX air-pocket as a seller because "the flip will hold." The dealer-long assumption itself fails exactly when it matters most (forced de-grossing in a crisis), and the published flip is crowded/front-run. Do not confuse GEX-pinning (modeled hedging) with the max-pain folk heuristic — the latter has no robust evidence base.

Honest edge — Falsifiable test: log your GEX-conditioned adjustments and compare realized vol / hit-rate in +GEX vs −GEX regimes over ≥40 tagged sessions. If the −GEX-defensive bucket doesn't show materially fewer large drawdowns, you have no edge. Verdict: Pro-only as a standalone signal (crowded, inference-laden); Skill-dependent and modestly useful strictly as a defensive regime filter that tells you when to TRADE LESS.

L4 · Professional

Dealer Hedging Regimes 造市商避險機制（伽瑪翻轉／Vanna・Charm）

How the inferred dealer book mechanically reshapes price action — the gamma-flip pivot, vanna (IV-driven) and charm (time-driven) hedging flows — read as HEURISTICS that depend on net dealer inventory and spot-vs-strikes, never as laws.

Pro-only
Prereq: Second-order Greeks (vanna/charm) · GEX/DEX · The Greeks

① 機制 Mechanism

This module APPLIES the second-order Greeks that L3-8 owns — and tells the identical signed story; if these two modules ever disagree, L3-8 is the source of truth. The canonical index setup: customers/hedgers BUY index puts → dealers are NET SHORT puts; a short put carries positive delta, so dealers hold SHORT stock as the hedge. Three modeled flows ride on top of that book: (1) Gamma regime & the flip. Above the zero-gamma level the (assumed-long-gamma) dealer hedges against moves → compression/pinning; below it the (assumed-short-gamma) dealer hedges with moves → trend/air-pocket. (2) Vanna = ∂delta/∂σ (equivalently ∂vega/∂spot). Rising IV makes a put's (negative) delta more negative; because the dealer is SHORT that put, the dealer's delta becomes more POSITIVE (longer) as IV rises — so the dealer SELLS stock to re-neutralize. (3) Charm = ∂delta/∂time, the hedging drift as options decay. All three are directional only conditional on the sign and size of net dealer inventory — flip the assumed inventory and every arrow reverses.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

No standalone payoff — this is a behavioral model of the tape. Its practical output is a conditional expectation of price path. The signed chain, stated identically to L3-8:

  • Customer position → long index puts.
  • Dealer book → short puts, hedged SHORT stock (short put = positive delta).
  • IV UP → vanna makes dealer delta more positive (longer) → dealer SELLS stock = downside ACCELERANT (and vol-up usually coincides with price-down, so it compounds).
  • IV DOWN (market calms) → dealer delta falls → dealer BUYS BACK stock → upward drift = the "VANNA RALLY" / grind-up.
  • Charm in calm (spot above the put strikes) → short-put delta decays toward 0 with time → dealer BUYS BACK stock into expiry / over weekends = CHARM support (the pre-OPEX / Friday drift up).

NET: IV-down or time-passing-in-a-calm-tape → dealers BUY (supportive); IV-up → dealers SELL (accelerant). Positive-gamma + falling IV + post-OPEX → low-vol grind (favors short premium); negative-gamma + rising IV → trend/gap (favors long convexity, punishes short premium). Vanna/charm tailwinds are real but second-order and unstable — they evaporate the instant IV or dealer inventory flips.

Vanna/charm dealer-flow for a dominant short-put book (dealers sold puts, hold short stock as hedge). Top arm (teal): IV down or time passing shrinks the short-put delta, so the short hedge is bought back, dealers BUY stock and the tape grinds up. Bottom arm (red): IV up (usually with price down) grows the put delta, dealers SELL more stock to re-hedge, accelerating the move down. Heuristic only, signs flip with net dealer inventory.
vannaIV↑ → dealer SELLS (accelerant); IV↓ → dealer BUYS (vanna rally)
charmcalm + spot above strikes → dealer BUYS into OPEX/weekend
flipregime pivot; every sign depends on net inventory

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

This is a pure second-order-Greeks lens. Gamma sets the regime (compress vs amplify). Vanna couples vol to delta — the reason a VIX spike mechanically generates self-reinforcing dealer SELLING (longer book → sell), and a calm VIX bleed-lower generates supportive BUYING (shorter book → buy back). Note the moneyness caveat on vanna's sign, stated exactly as L3-8 owns it: vanna (∂Δ/∂σ) is not a single OTM sign — it depends on moneyness AND option type/convention. For a long call it is generally POSITIVE for OTM and NEGATIVE for ITM, crossing ~zero just above the ATM forward and fading toward zero deep OTM and deep ITM; a long put's vanna-by-moneyness is the mirror. So the dealer-flow direction depends on where spot sits relative to the strikes (the signed cross-strike vanna profile, i.e. skew), not just the IV move. Charm couples time to delta — the reason hedging flows cluster into expiry. Magnitudes scale with aggregate OI near spot and with how concentrated strikes are; they are largest in big index OPEX and around large dealer positions. Every one of these is an estimate of an aggregate, not a tradable Greek you can see.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: a calm, post-event, positive-gamma + declining-IV tape into OPEX week — the vanna+charm "melt-up" setup (IV-down → dealer buys; charm → dealer buys into expiry) that supports a modest long-delta or short-put tilt. It also works in reverse as a warning: negative-gamma + rising IV says "do not be short premium / vol here" because IV-up means dealers are SELLING. It fails when: dealer inventory is mis-assumed (every sign flips), a fresh catalyst overrides flow (flows are dwarfed by macro shocks), IV doesn't behave as the model expects, or the level is so widely watched it's front-run. These are heuristics with a real microstructure basis, not deterministic laws.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Vanna/charm melt-ups — and their limit, Aug 5 2024 (as-of). The "grind-up into OPEX on falling VIX" pattern (IV-down → dealer buy-back) is well-documented in index tapes through 2023–2024 quiet stretches. The instructive counter-case is Aug 5 2024: the same vanna machinery ran in reverse — a VIX intraday spike to 65.73 (close 38.57, prior 23.39, per Cboe) flipped IV-up vanna into self-reinforcing dealer SELLING below the gamma flip (longer book on IV-up → sell), producing the air-pocket. The dealer-flow magnitude is an inferred ESTIMATE and the move was multi-causal (yen-carry unwind; illiquid pre-market SPX options distorting the VIX calc — VX futures stayed below 35 while spot VIX spiked). Lesson: vanna/charm are a tailwind in calm regimes (IV-down buys, charm buys) and a downside accelerant in stress (IV-up sells) — and a single macro catalyst (BoJ hike) overrode the gentle flow entirely.

Sources: Cboe VIX daily history (Aug 5 2024); Cboe VX futures settlements; contemporaneous dealer-flow commentary (SpotGamma / SqueezeMetrics).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked usage ticket — Underlying SPX, OPEX week, as-of (illustrative). Read: gamma positive above flip, IVR 28 and falling (IV-down → modeled dealer buy-back), no FOMC/CPI in the next 3 sessions, large call OI at a strike +1.5% above. Decision: a planned 7-DTE short put credit spread is entered at the open (rather than waiting) to ride the modeled vanna (IV-down buy) + charm (buy into expiry) grind into Friday; short 20Δ put / long 10Δ, net credit $0.55, max loss $4.45/spread, hard stop if IV jumps >3 vol pts (vanna-flip-to-selling trigger) OR spot breaks flip. The flow only changed the timing/conviction of entry, not the structure or size.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Same liquidity bar as GEX — only big index/ETF tapes have a clean enough aggregate book for vanna/charm to dominate: options ADV >100k, OI >1M, monthly OPEX with large standing OI. AVOID single-names (idiosyncratic flow swamps dealer hedging). FREE sources: SpotGamma educational vanna/charm explainers + chart snapshots; SqueezeMetrics daily aggregate gamma; CBOE OI for DIY. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): SPX/SPY (cleanest, especially OPEX week) and QQQ (clean index tape).

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: over-fitting a calm-regime heuristic into stress. The same vanna that grinds the market up on falling IV (dealer buys back) becomes a downside accelerant on an IV spike (dealer's short-put book lengthens → dealer SELLS), and charm's "drift to the big strike" gets overwhelmed by any real catalyst. Mis-assuming the dealer inventory sign reverses every prediction — these are heuristics that flip entirely with net dealer inventory and with moneyness (a long call's vanna is generally positive for OTM and negative for ITM, ~zero just above ATM; a long put's profile is the mirror — there is NO single OTM vanna sign), exactly per L3-8. People who built a melt-up muscle memory in 2023–24 got steamrolled when the regime flipped in Aug 2024. For the signed theory itself, see L3-8 — this module must always match it.

Honest edge — Falsifiable test: tag entries as "vanna/charm-tailwind present vs absent" and compare same-strategy P&L and timing-slippage across ≥40 trades; if the tailwind bucket shows no better fill/path, the flow read added nothing. Verdict: Pro-only as a thesis; Skill-dependent as a timing/path overlay — it modulates WHEN and HOW MUCH defense you run, never standalone direction.

L4 · Professional

0DTE Dynamics 當日到期（盤中伽瑪與執行風險）

Same-day-expiry options where gamma is enormous and theta brutal — for retail this is "picking up pennies in front of a steamroller" UNLESS the trade is defined-risk and catalyst-aware.

Crowded-low-edge
Prereq: The Greeks · Liquidity & execution · Dealer hedging regimes

① 機制 Mechanism

A 0DTE option expires the same trading day. With hours to expiry, an at-the-money option's gamma is extreme and its theta is violent — delta can swing from 0.10 to 0.90 on a small spot move, and time value evaporates by the hour. You are trading almost pure realized-vol-over-the-next-few-hours. Two distinct uses: (a) short 0DTE premium (sell the rich same-day decay) — collect small theta, but you are short extreme gamma, so one fast move can multiply the loss faster than any other retail structure; (b) long 0DTE as a cheap catalyst lottery — defined max loss, but theta crushes it to zero by close if the move doesn't come. Because 0DTE flow is now a huge share of index volume, it also feeds back into intraday dealer gamma (link to L4-2), tightening pins and sharpening air-pockets.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Shown: a defined-risk 0DTE put credit spread with the short strike AT the money — short the 100 put / long the 98 put, spot 100, ~hours to expiry — the most visceral way to see same-day gamma. This is the ONLY 0DTE shape retail should consider, and even here the ATM short strike is deliberately aggressive to make the risk obvious. Width is 2 pts. At fractional DTE the T-now curve sits almost on the expiry kink: there is essentially no time-value buffer, so the position is its terminal payoff almost immediately, and the P&L hinges entirely on whether spot finishes above or below the 100 short strike. A naked short 0DTE put at 100 would keep the same small max-credit but carry a near-unbounded same-day loss as gamma explodes below the strike.

+$0.90max profit = net credit (illustrative, ATM ~½ of width)
−$1.10max loss = width − credit = 2.00 − 0.90 (defined)
99.10breakeven = short K − credit = 100 − 0.90

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

0DTE is the gamma/theta extreme of the surface. ATM gamma is the largest of any expiry — tiny spot moves cause large delta swings, so a short 0DTE position's effective delta whips violently. With the short strike sitting AT the money (spot 100, short 100 put), you are parked exactly on the gamma peak: a small move through the strike flips the spread's P&L from near-max-profit to near-max-loss in a matter of points. Theta is enormous and front-loaded into the final hours (the seller's only friend). Vega is small (little time for IV to matter) — 0DTE is a realized-vol / gamma trade, not a vega trade. The defining feature: you cannot "wait it out" — there is no later expiry to roll into within the trade, so gamma risk is acute and continuous until close. This is also where genuine pin risk bites: real assignment uncertainty when spot finishes right at a short strike (a settlement/exercise problem), which is NOT the dubious max-pain folk theory — keep the two separate.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE defensible cell for retail: defined-risk, catalyst-aware, small. Either a defined-risk credit spread on a calm range-day where you've checked there is NO scheduled catalyst (CPI/FOMC/major earnings) inside the day, OR a cheap long lottery sized as a write-off when you have a specific same-day catalyst view. An ATM short strike like the 100 put shown maximizes credit but also maximizes the chance of being run over — most survivable 0DTE spreads sit further OTM. It fails — catastrophically — as undefined-risk short premium: the high win-rate lulls you, then one CPI surprise or air-pocket delivers a loss many times the cumulative credits collected (the "pennies vs steamroller" tail). It is also crowded: 0DTE is now a dominant flow, so any naive "sell theta" edge is heavily competed and arbitraged.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

The 0DTE share of SPX volume (denominator-precise, Cboe). 0DTE SPX options were ~47% of SPX options volume in 2024 and 59% of full-year-2025 SPX volume (ADV record ~2.3M contracts) — verified via Cboe / Traders Magazine. Denominator matters and is routinely conflated: that ~47–59% is the share of SPX-index options, by contracts. Across all US-listed options, 0DTE was a much smaller ~24.1% in 2025 (up from ~21.5% in 2024, per OCC/Cboe). Cboe/JPM/Nomura figures use different bases (SPX vs total; contracts vs notional) and are not interchangeable. The execution lesson is Aug 5 2024: on an air-pocket day, a 0DTE short-premium book sees its small daily credits dwarfed by a single intraday gap (VIX intraday 65.73, close 38.57) — the steamroller arriving.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
StructureDefined-risk ONLY (credit/debit spread); never naked 0DTE
