Worked trade ticket — matched exactly to the chart above (per-share, single-expiry, illustrative). Underlying spot 100, IV 30%, 0DTE (~hours to expiry). ATM put credit spread: short the 100 put / long the 98 put, width 2.00 pts, 1 contract. Net credit $0.90/share ($90/spread). Max profit = net credit = $0.90 ($90) if spot finishes ≥ 100. Max loss = width − credit = 2.00 − 0.90 = $1.10 ($110) if spot finishes ≤ 98; BPR ≈ max loss (defined). Breakeven = short strike − credit = 100 − 0.90 = 99.10. Target: buy back at $0.45 (50% of credit). Stop: buy back at $1.80 (2× credit) or on touch of the 100 short strike. Hard flat near the close regardless. The 0DTE point made literal: with the short strike AT the money and only hours left, gamma and theta are violent — a small spot move past 100 flips this from +$0.90 toward −$1.10 almost instantly. That is why same-day short premium is pennies-in-front-of-a-steamroller WITHOUT this capped, pre-budgeted defined risk and a hard time-stop.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

0DTE is only viable where same-day expiries exist AND spreads are penny-tight: SPX, SPY, QQQ (daily expiries) and a few mega-caps with daily/near-daily expiries. Screen: bid/ask <5% of mid on the strikes you trade, OI >5k at the strike, options ADV >100k; AVOID any 0DTE on illiquid names (the spread alone eats the edge), and AVOID days with a scheduled in-session catalyst. PREFER SPX over SPY for 0DTE: cash-settled (no early assignment / pin), Section 1256 60/40 tax, no dividend risk. FREE screener: broker chain (Thinkorswim / IBKR) filtered to today's expiry; Cboe daily-expiry calendar. Candidates as-of 2026-06-17 (est.): SPX (best — cash-settled) and SPY/QQQ (tight but physically-settled, watch pin).

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha is the negatively-skewed P&L of short 0DTE gamma: a string of small wins builds false confidence, then a single intraday gap (a CPI shock, an air-pocket in a −gamma regime) delivers a loss that erases months of credits — and with no time left, you cannot roll or wait it out. The ATM 100/98 spread above shows it in miniature: a few points through the short strike swings the whole position from +$0.90 to −$1.10. Undefined-risk short 0DTE is the fastest retail account-killer in this whole dashboard. SPY/QQQ add physical-settlement pin/assignment risk at the close (genuine pin risk — assignment uncertainty at a short strike — not the max-pain heuristic).

Honest edge — Falsifiable test: track cumulative credits collected vs your single worst-day loss over ≥50 sessions; if max drawdown ≥ several months of credits, the "high win-rate" is a mirage — you're selling cheap tail. Verdict: Crowded-low-edge (0DTE is now a dominant, heavily-competed flow). Survivable for retail ONLY as defined-risk + catalyst-aware + hard-stopped; as naked short premium it is negative-edge after the tail.

Sources: Cboe (SPX 0DTE volume share, daily-expiry calendar); Traders Magazine (2025 SPX 0DTE ADV ~2.3M); OCC/Cboe (all-US-listed 0DTE share ~24.1% 2025); Cboe VIX history (Aug 5 2024 intraday 65.73 / close 38.57). Payoff/KPI/ticket figures are single-expiry per-share Black–Scholes, illustrative (spot 100, IV 30%, 0DTE).

L4 · Professional

OPEX / Quad-Witching, Max Pain & Pin Risk 到期日／四巫日・最大痛點・釘住風險

The monthly options-expiration calendar creates a real, mechanical force (dealer hedges roll off, gamma collapses) — but "max pain" is a crowded post-hoc story, while "pin risk" is a genuine assignment hazard at your own short strike.

Crowded-low-edge
Prereq: Mechanics, assignment & pin · GEX as regime filter · Dealer hedging regimes

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down: monthly equity/index options and futures expire on the third Friday. Four times a year — March, June, September, December — index futures, index options and single-stock options all expire together; this is "quad-witching" (really triple-witching since single-stock futures were delisted in 2020). 2026 dates: Mar 20, Jun 18 (the third Friday is Jun 19 = the Juneteenth market holiday, so expiry rolls to the preceding Thursday, Jun 18), Sep 18, Dec 18. The real structural effect: a huge block of dealer gamma exposure expires and disappears, so dealers stop pinning and the market often gets freer to move the following week — this is why "OPEX week vs post-OPEX week" seasonality exists, not because of any single magic price.

Now separate two ideas that retail constantly conflates: (a) Max pain — the strike at which the aggregate dollar value of expiring options (across the whole chain) would be smallest, i.e. the strike that maximizes pain for option buyers. It is computed from open interest, which is stale and signed by no one; the claim "price gets pulled to max pain" is a dubious, crowded, post-hoc heuristic — prices land near it sometimes simply because OI clusters near spot. (b) Pin risk — a real hazard: when the underlying closes at or pennies from a strike you are short, you do not know after the close whether you'll be assigned, so you don't know your Monday delta. That is genuine and lives in your own position, independent of any market-wide "pull."

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

This is not a single-leg structure — the payoff that matters is the short option you are carrying INTO expiry sitting at the strike. The danger is the post-close window: a short call at strike K, spot closes at K. Whether you wake up flat or short 100 shares per contract is a coin-flip on counterparty exercise notices. The chart below shows that short-call's expiry kink; the gotcha lives exactly at the corner.

$45max profit (premium kept, per contract)
Unboundedmax loss (naked call) — pin caps nothing
Pin zone ±$0.50assignment uncertainty band at K

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

The defining feature of expiry day is gamma → vertical at the strike. As DTE→0 and spot→K, a short option's gamma and theta both explode in magnitude (the short option is −gamma, +theta): delta whips from ~0 to ~±1 over pennies of spot. So your position delta is wildly unstable in the pin zone — the single most dangerous Greek state in retail trading. Across the whole market, the aggregate of all expiring contracts means dealer net gamma steps down discontinuously at the open after OPEX, which is the mechanism behind post-OPEX-week regime shifts (more freedom to trend). Vega is near zero at expiry — this is purely a gamma/theta and assignment event, not a vol event.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE legitimate use is a regime/timing filter, not an entry signal: (1) Defensive — flatten or close any short option that risks pinning before the Friday close; never carry naked pin risk over an OPEX weekend. (2) Tactical context — treat post-OPEX week as a period where dealer pinning is weaker, so trend/breakout setups have marginally more room (a coincident filter, combine with GEX). It FAILS as an alpha source the moment you trade toward a max-pain print: the number moves as OI changes, it's published by everyone, and the "pull" is unfalsifiable. View × IV × event cell: any view, into the third Friday, with open short options near the money → act on pin risk; ignore max pain as a price target.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Quad-witching, Sep 20 2024 (verified date — third Friday). ~$5.1T notional of options were estimated to expire (SpotGamma/Goldman desk estimates — est., providers' notional methods differ). SPX traded in a tight range into the cash close then the following week saw a clean directional move as that gamma rolled off — the textbook "pin Friday, free Monday" pattern. The lesson is structural, not predictive: the visible effect (range compression on the day, expansion after) is repeatable; the invisible effect ("max pain dragged it to strike X") is not — that same week multiple max-pain trackers disagreed on the SPX max-pain strike by 50+ points. Pin risk, by contrast, was concrete: anyone short an SPY call at a round strike that Friday faced genuine Monday-delta uncertainty.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
Entry IVR gateN/A — this is a calendar/management overlay, not a standalone entry
Delta / strikeFlag any short leg within ±1.5% of spot into the third Friday
DTE windowAct in the 2-day window before monthly/quad expiry
Profit targetClose short options at ≥80% of max profit rather than carry pin risk
Stop / time-stopHard time-stop: be FLAT of near-money short legs by Friday 15:30 ET
SizingPin risk is binary — size as if you may be assigned 100% of contracts overnight; ensure BPR/cash covers it
Defend triggerActionResulting structureNew risk
Short call pinning at K on Fri PMBuy-to-close for pennies before the closeFlat / no overnight deltaPay a small debit; give up last cents of theta
Don't-know-if-assigned over the weekendBuy/sell the offsetting stock Mon at open if a notice appearsNet flat once reconciledGap risk Fri-close→Mon-open on the unhedged 100 shares
Want to stay short premium through OPEXRoll the near-money leg to next cycle / out of the moneyNew short option, no pinRe-establishes vega/gamma; resets the clock
Worked management ticket — Carrying 5 short SPY $540 calls (covered, against 500 sh) into Sep-2024 quad-witching. SPY trades $539.80 at 15:25 ET Friday. Pin zone. Decision: BTC all 5 at $0.06 = $30 total debit, vs the alternative of unknown Monday assignment on 500 shares with a weekend gap. You forfeit ~$30 of residual theta to convert a binary overnight delta into a known flat book. Net: kept ~95% of the week's premium, eliminated pin risk. This is the trade — buying back cheap pin risk, not predicting max pain.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen for pin exposure, not for a tradeable max-pain edge. Runnable filter (as-of 2026-06-17): for each open short leg, flag if |spot − strike| / spot < 1.5% AND DTE ≤ 2 AND it's a monthly/quad expiry. Prefer cash-settled index (SPX, XSP) for any position you'd carry through OPEX — no physical assignment, no early-exercise pin (a structural fix, not a screen result). Free tools: OCC expiration calendar for the third-Friday dates; your broker's risk/positions page for which legs are near the money; Cboe.com for quad-witching notional context. Candidate underlyings where retail most often gets pinned (high single-name OI at round strikes, est.): SPY, AAPL, NVDA — all dense round-strike OI as-of 2026-06-17 (est.).

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The blow-up is the weekend pin: spot closes exactly at your short strike, you assume you expired worthless, and Monday you discover you were assigned 100 shares/contract — now naked-long or naked-short into a gap. American-style equity options can also be exercised early the night of expiry against you. The second gotcha is believing max pain is a magnet and fading price toward it; that's a crowded, unfalsifiable trade that hands you the wrong delta at the worst moment.

Honest edge — Confirm/deny metric: backtest whether entering toward published max-pain on Wednesday and closing Friday produces positive expectancy after slippage across ≥100 expiries. It does not survive costs (the print drifts; everyone sees it). Verdict: Crowded-low-edge as a directional signal. The ONLY genuine value here is defensive — pin-risk management — which is operational hygiene, not alpha.

L4 · Professional

Variance, Correlation & Dispersion 變異數・相關性・離散交易

Index volatility is structurally cheaper than the average of its members because of diversification — the dispersion trade (short index vol / long single-name vol) harvests the gap between implied and realized correlation, and it is overwhelmingly an institutional trade.

Pro-only
Prereq: Variance vs vol & the VRP · IV & the surface · Risk, sizing & margin

① 機制 Mechanism

Top-down intuition first: an index moves less than its average member because members partly cancel each other — that cancellation IS diversification, and its strength is governed by correlation. The math makes it exact. Index variance is:

σ²_index = Σᵢ wᵢ²σᵢ² + Σᵢ≠ⱼ wᵢwⱼρᵢⱼσᵢσⱼ

The first sum is the members' own (idiosyncratic) variance; the second sum is the cross-terms — every pairwise correlation ρᵢⱼ. If ρ→0 the cross-terms vanish and the index is far calmer than its parts; if ρ→1 the index variance approaches the squared weighted-average vol. So index implied vol embeds an implied correlation. You can invert the formula to back out the market's implied correlation from index IV and the single-name IVs (this is what Cboe's COR1M/COR3M implied-correlation indices, the old "ICJ/JCJ," publish). The dispersion trade: SHORT index volatility and LONG a basket of single-name volatility. You profit when single names move a lot relative to the index — i.e. when realized correlation comes in below the implied correlation you sold. You are, net, short correlation.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

There is no clean single-expiry payoff line — the position is a portfolio of straddles/variance swaps (short index straddle vs long ~20-50 single-name straddles, vega-weighted to be roughly index-vega-neutral), so the engine cannot draw it. The P&L is a function of realized correlation vs the implied correlation entered: P&L ≈ Σ (single-name realized var) − (index realized var) − net premium. Breakeven is the implied correlation level you transacted at. The concept SVG shows the structural gap: single-name IVs fanned high, index IV pinned low, and the correlation wedge between them that you are selling.

The dispersion trade: sell one index straddle (short index vol, red) against a basket of single-name straddles (long single-name vol, teal) — net a short position on implied correlation. The payoff (blue) is positive when realized correlation sits below the implied level baked into index options (members move independently) and turns negative in a correlated crash where everything sells off together. A capital-intensive, pro-only structure.
Capped-ishmax profit ≈ if ρ_realized→0
Large / "short corr"max loss when a crash slams ρ_realized→1
Implied ρ enteredbreakeven correlation

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Constructed to be roughly vega-neutral at the index level (you sold index vega, bought single-name vega in equal measure) — the residual exposure is the correlation Greek (a.k.a. "cega"/dCorr), which is the whole point. Net you are short index gamma, long single-name gamma. In calm, idiosyncratic-driven tapes that's beautiful (single names wiggle, index doesn't, you collect). In a correlated crash the relationship inverts violently: everything moves together (ρ→1), your short-index straddle loses far more than your long single-name straddles gain — short correlation behaves like short tail-risk. Theta is roughly netted out by construction; the danger is not theta, it's a correlation spike.

Greek profile of a dispersion (short-correlation) book: long basket vega offsets short index vega so net vega and net gamma sit near zero — but the book carries a large, dominant SHORT-CORRELATION exposure. The true residual risk is rising realized co-movement (ρ), not volatility level.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell: implied correlation is rich (high COR index) AND your view is that the market will be driven by single-name/idiosyncratic news rather than macro shocks (earnings season, stock-picker regimes, low-VIX grind). It works when realized correlation undershoots the implied you sold. It fails — badly — in any macro risk-off: a Fed shock, a growth scare, an Aug-2024-style unwind sends every stock down together, ρ_realized spikes toward 1, and the short-index leg detonates. So dispersion is a bet against systemic risk; the IV-vs-realized distinction here is really an implied-correlation-vs-realized-correlation distinction.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

2023-2024 dispersion boom, then the Aug-5-2024 unwind. Through 2023 and H1-2024, implied correlation sat near multi-year lows (the "AI mega-cap idiosyncrasy" regime) and dispersion (short-correlation) was one of the most crowded, profitable institutional vol trades — widely covered as banks' hottest desk trade. Then on Aug 5 2024 the yen-carry unwind hit: VIX printed an intraday/indicative high around ~65 (not the close — it closed dramatically lower, ~38; the ~65 figure was an early-session indicative value during a thin tape, and the carry unwind was a driver, not the sole cause). Correlation spiked as everything sold off together, and crowded short-correlation/dispersion books took sharp losses in a single session. Lesson: dispersion is short systemic risk; it pays steadily then gives a chunk back in one correlated crash — classic negatively-skewed P&L.

Sources: Cboe COR1M/COR3M implied-correlation index methodology & history (cboe.com); Reuters/FT coverage of the Aug 5 2024 yen-carry unwind and the VIX intraday spike (~65 indicative) vs ~38 close.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Illustrative trade ticket (institutional-scale; reconstructed est. — NOT a retail order) — Thesis: COR3M implied correlation at a high percentile, earnings season ahead. Short 1 SPX 45-DTE ATM straddle (sell index vega) vs long ATM straddles on the ~30 largest SPX names, each sized so total single-name vega ≈ index vega (index-vega-neutral). Net structure is short correlation. Breakeven = the implied correlation transacted. Target: COR3M down ~7 pts → close for gain. Stop: VIX +10 intraday → buy back the SPX straddle immediately. No-real-money retail gate: this is undefined-risk, short-tail and properly run only with portfolio margin and (ideally) variance-swap access — capital/margin and execution make it Pro-only. It is printed here so a retail trader understands what the COR index and the "crowded dispersion trade" headlines actually mean, not as something to put on.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

First "trade" to try — PAPER / OBSERVE-ONLY, no real money. Dispersion is Pro-only: the real trade needs a 30-50-name basket plus portfolio margin and ideally variance-swap access, and the listed-option proxy (short index straddle vs a basket of single-name straddles) carries high basis risk and demands constant delta/vega rebalancing — so for a retail account the first step is not a live position. Instead, build a correlation-tracking WORKSHEET and paper-trade it.

The worksheet (implied vs realized correlation). As-of 2026-06-17:

  • Implied correlation: log Cboe COR1M / COR3M implied-correlation indices daily (free on cboe.com); record their percentile vs the last 1-year range, and pair with the VIX percentile. (Or invert the formula yourself: from index IV and the single-name IVs, back out implied ρ.)
  • Realized correlation: over a trailing window (e.g. 21 trading days) compute pairwise realized correlations of your chosen basket and roll them into a basket-average realized ρ, using the same identity σ²_index = Σᵢ wᵢ²σᵢ² + Σᵢ≠ⱼ wᵢwⱼρᵢⱼσᵢσⱼ solved for the average ρ. This is the number the trade is really about.
  • The signal: chart implied ρ minus subsequent realized ρ over many overlapping windows. A persistently positive, fat gap is the correlation risk premium you would be selling; track whether it survives a stress (does the gap go sharply negative when VIX spikes?).
  • Paper-trade it: when the worksheet flags rich implied ρ, write down the hypothetical short-index-straddle-vs-basket-of-single-name-straddles structure, mark it daily, and force yourself to log the delta/vega rebalancing it would have required — that rebalancing burden and the basis between your 2-3-name proxy and the index is the lesson.

Basket to watch (for the worksheet, not a live order). Pair high single-name-vol idiosyncratic members against their index/ETF, screening members for OI > 2,000, bid/ask < 5% of mid, and an upcoming idiosyncratic catalyst within DTE. Candidate idiosyncratic names (est. as-of 2026-06-17): NVDA, TSLA, SMCI against QQQ. Free data: cboe.com (COR1M/COR3M), Barchart (correlation/vol), market-chameleon (vol surface). Only after the worksheet shows you understand the implied-vs-realized-correlation gap, the basis risk, and the rebalancing cost — and only with portfolio margin / variance-swap access — does any real-money version belong on the table.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The blow-up is a correlation-to-one event: every diversification benefit you sold evaporates in a crash, the short-index leg overwhelms the long basket, and losses are convex and fast (Aug-5-2024 in miniature). Secondary gotchas: execution (dozens of wide single-name spreads — bid/ask is your biggest, most certain cost), basis risk (a retail 3-name proxy is not the index), and financing/margin for the undefined-risk short-index leg.

Honest edge — Confirm/deny metric: is implied correlation (COR3M) persistently above subsequent realized correlation over your holding window, net of the cost of running 30+ legs? At institutional scale and cost the correlation risk premium is real but thin; at retail cost and with a 2-3-name proxy, slippage + basis risk eats it. Verdict: Pro-only. Genuine premium exists, but it is captured by desks with variance-swap access and near-zero execution cost — not by a retail multi-leg basket.

L4 · Professional

Box / Jelly Roll / Conversion-Reversal 盒式價差・果凍卷・轉換套利

Pure put-call-parity structures that synthesize a fixed cash flow — a box is a synthetic loan, a jelly roll is the financing of a calendar, a conversion/reversal locks stock against synthetic stock; they trade interest rates, not direction, and carry no retail edge.

Pro-only
Prereq: Put-call parity & synthetics · Mechanics, assignment & pin · Rho / rates / carry

① 機制 Mechanism

All three are children of put-call parity C − P = S − PV(K) − PV(div). A long box = a bull call spread + a bear put spread at the same two strikes (K1<K2) — equivalently long K1 call / short K2 call / short K1 put / long K2 put. At expiry it always pays exactly the width (K2 − K1) × 100 regardless of where the stock lands — so it is a synthetic zero-coupon bond you OWN. You pay a debit today ≈ PV(K2 − K1), slightly below the width; the discount IS an interest rate, so a long box = you LENT cash (bought the bond) and earn the implied financing rate to expiry. A short box is the mirror: you take in a credit today and repay the fixed width at expiry — so a short box = you BORROWED cash at the implied rate. Institutions use boxes to borrow (short box) or lend (long box) cash synthetically at near-risk-free rates (often cheaper than a margin loan, and on SPX with 1256 tax treatment). A jelly roll is the difference between a call calendar and a put calendar at the same strike — it isolates the cost of carry / dividend + financing embedded between two expirations; it's how you trade or hedge the financing leg of a calendar. A conversion = long stock + long put + short call (same strike/expiry) = you hold the real shares fully hedged into a fixed payoff, i.e. a synthetic LONG bond → you LENT cash; a reversal (short stock + short put + long call) is the mirror, a synthetic SHORT bond → you BORROWED cash. All are delta-, gamma-, vega-neutral by construction — what's left is rho (rates) and dividend/early-exercise risk.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

A long box is the cleanest payoff in options: a flat horizontal line at (K2−K1)×100 at expiry, for any spot — there is no breakeven and no direction. Your "profit" is whether the width minus your debit beats the risk-free rate over the holding period. Note the box is a single-expiry, four-leg vertical structure (all legs share one expiration), so the engine can legitimately draw it — it shows the payoff collapsing to a constant.

$1,000fixed value at expiry (width $10 ×100)
Rate − funding"profit" = implied yield vs your cost
None (flat)breakeven / direction-free

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

By design Δ ≈ Γ ≈ V ≈ Θ ≈ 0 — the box/conversion/reversal cancels every first-order spot and vol Greek. The ONE live Greek is rho: the box's value is the present value of the width, so it moves with interest rates and time-to-expiry (it accretes toward the width like a bond pulling to par). The jelly roll's whole exposure is the differential carry (financing + dividends) between the two expiries. The hidden, non-Greek risk that dominates American-style underlyings is early-exercise / assignment on the short legs — which is exactly what breaks the "risk-free" assumption (see ⑧).

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE legitimate cell: you need synthetic financing — lend cash / earn a yield (long box: pay a debit now ≈ PV(width), collect the fixed width at expiry) or borrow cash cheaply (short box: take a credit now, repay the fixed width at expiry) — and you can do it on European, cash-settled options (SPX) where there is no early-assignment risk. It also "works" as a way to lock in a found arbitrage between parity-violating prices, but those are vanishingly rare and arbed by market makers in milliseconds. It fails the moment you (a) do it on American-style single-name options (early assignment breaks the lock), (b) misprice the financing (you "borrow" at a worse rate than your broker's margin), or (c) treat it as free money. There is no directional or vol edge here at all — it is a rates/financing instrument.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

The 1R0NYMAN Robinhood box-spread blow-up, January 2019 (verified). A WallStreetBets user ("1R0NYMAN") SHORTED a box spread on American-style options (UVXY) — i.e. took the credit now to borrow cash, owing the fixed width at expiry — believing the payoff "literally cannot go tits up." Robinhood's lenient margin treatment let him build a position far larger than his cash, the so-called "infinite leverage" effect. Reported facts (≥2 sources; est. on exact strikes/timing): account ~$5,000; the position was force-liquidated and ultimately left a loss of roughly ~$58,000 — many multiples of the account — after he had withdrawn ~$10,000 first. Robinhood banned box spreads on the app almost immediately afterward. Why it blew up: because he was short the box (he had borrowed via the credit), legs of an American-style structure got exercised against him early, so the supposedly self-financing, "risk-free" borrow was no longer a locked four-leg structure — he was left holding a naked, directional, deeply underwater leg with leverage stacked on top. Lesson: a box is only "risk-free" on European, cash-settled options; on American-style options, early assignment is the killer — it turns parity into a trap. (Note: a separate, later 2019 Robinhood "infinite leverage" covered-call glitch — selling covered calls against borrowed margin, ~$100k aggregate across ~20 users, est. — is a different bug; do not conflate it with this box-spread loss.)

Sources: 1R0NYMAN WallStreetBets post / thread (Jan 2019); contemporaneous coverage (Business Insider / Bloomberg, "Robinhood bans box spreads," 2019).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

First trade = PAPER / OBSERVE-ONLY. Do NOT put real capital on a box unless you have portfolio margin AND demonstrated SPX-box-specific competence (you have priced the SPX rate curve, modeled the implied yield, and traded the structure on paper). This is a pure financing trade — it has no directional and no volatility edge for retail — so the only thing you are "winning" is a few basis points vs your margin rate, against which any execution slip or modeling error works directly. Treat the worked ticket below as a calculation to observe and reproduce on paper, not a buy order.

Worked ticket (synthetic LEND — long box, the only sane retail use) — Long box on SPX (European, cash-settled, 1256-taxed), strikes 5000/5100, 180 DTE. Width = $100 → fixed expiry value $10,000 per box. You pay a debit of, say, $9,760 now (≈ PV of the $10,000 width) → you "lend" $9,760 today to receive the fixed $10,000 in 180 days = an implied annualized yield ≈ ~5.0% (illustrative; exact debit set by the SPX rate curve). This is the lend / buy-the-bond direction: debit out now, fixed width back at expiry. (The mirror — a short box — would take in a ~$9,760 credit now and repay the $10,000 width at expiry = synthetic borrow at that same implied rate.) No early-assignment risk because SPX is European, cash-settled. Δ/Γ/Θ/V all ≈ 0; the only risk is rates/credit and execution. This is a cash-management trade, not a way to make alpha.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen for European, cash-settled, liquid underlyings ONLY: SPX and XSP (and NDX) — never single-name American options for a box. Numeric gate (as-of 2026-06-17): both verticals' bid/ask < 2% of mid (rate-trades die on spread), OI > 1,000 per strike, and the implied box yield must beat your margin rate by > 0.5% annualized after commissions. Compute the implied yield with any options-pricing sheet or Cboe chain data (free). Named candidates: SPX (deepest box market, used by funds for synthetic financing), XSP (1/10th SPX, smaller size). The "screen" is really a rate comparison, not a stock pick.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

Lead risk — the 2019 Robinhood "infinite leverage" box blow-up (1R0NYMAN, ⑤). The catastrophic gotcha is putting a box on American-style options: a short leg gets exercised against you early, the four-leg lock breaks, and you are suddenly holding a naked directional position — and if it was financed with stacked margin, with multiples of your account at risk. Early assignment on American-style (and any index-adjacent American) options is the killer; the only structural defense is to use European, cash-settled SPX/XSP/NDX, where assignment cannot happen before expiry. Secondary gotchas: pin/settlement risk at expiry on American legs, execution slippage (a rate trade has razor-thin margin; bid/ask can erase the entire yield advantage), and broker liquidation if margin math goes wrong intraday. There is no offsetting upside — for retail this is a financing trade with no directional and no volatility edge.

Honest edge — Confirm/deny metric: does the implied box yield (after commissions) beat your actual margin borrow rate, AND are the options European so the lock cannot break? If yes, it's a legitimate financing tool with zero alpha; if the options are American, expected edge is negative because early-assignment risk is uncompensated. Verdict: Pro-only / no-edge. Useful only as cheap synthetic financing on SPX — a cash-management trick, never a source of return.

L4 · Professional

Capstone — Dealer-Positioning-Aware Playbook 整合戰術・做市商定位整合交易計畫

The synthesis: layer your own fundamental view + a dealer-gamma REGIME read (GEX) + the SKEW shape + the IVR-and-liquidity gate into ONE single-expiry structure where every leg is justified by a different edge — and execute it end-to-end.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: GEX/DEX · Dealer hedging regimes · Skew · IV & surface · Risk & sizing

① 機制 Mechanism

This module fuses four independent reads into one single-expiry trade so that no single inference has to be right. The discipline is a four-filter stack, top-down: (1) Fundamental / directional view — your directional/range thesis on the underlying (the only filter you actually have an information edge on). (2) Dealer-gamma regime (GEX) — is the tape likely pin-y (assumed dealer-long-gamma, +GEX, mean-reverting) or trend-amplifying (−GEX)? Use it ONLY as context / a regime filter, remembering GEX is an inference, not a measurement, providers sign it differently, and OI is stale. (3) Skew — are downside puts expensively bid (steep skew) so you'd rather sell downside premium, or is skew flat/cheap? This tells you which side is richest to sell. (4) IVR + liquidity — is implied vol high in its own one-year range (IVR), so there is genuinely something rich to sell not just to pay, AND is the chain liquid enough (tight bid/ask, deep OI) that you can actually enter and exit the spread without the spread cost eating the credit? The output: choose which side to sell from skew, choose whether there is premium worth selling at all from IVR, confirm you can trade it cleanly from liquidity, choose direction from your thesis, and choose defined vs undefined risk from the gamma regime. Because this is a single-expiry vertical, every leg shares one expiry — so tenor/term-structure is NOT a filter here. Term structure (contango/backwardation across expiries) is a calendar/diagonal edge, not a single-expiry one; it lives in the multi-expiry modules (see L2-6 and L3-5), and conflating SPX-options term structure with the VIX-futures curve is a classic error this stack deliberately avoids. Every leg earns its place.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

The capstone structure is regime-dependent; the worked example below is a single-expiry put credit spread in a +GEX (pin) regime with steep skew and elevated, liquid IVR — you sell the expensive downside put, buy a cheaper further-OTM put for defined risk, in the pinning regime that favors range-bound mean-reversion. The engine draws this single-expiry defined-risk payoff; in a −GEX (trend) regime you'd instead reach for a long debit spread or long premium (different card).

$90max profit (net credit, per spread)
$410max loss (width $5 − credit)×100
$94.10 / ~70%breakeven / est. POP

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

The worked put-credit-spread is net long delta (you want spot up/flat), short gamma (you're short the nearer put — hurts on a fast drop), positive theta (you collected net credit — time decay works for you), and short vega (you sold the higher-IV strike — IV expansion hurts). Crucially, you deliberately chose this short-gamma/short-vega profile because the GEX regime says dealers are long gamma and likely to dampen moves, the skew says downside IV is rich to sell, and IVR says implied vol sits high enough in its range that there is real premium to harvest (and liquidity says you can actually realize it). The Greeks are the consequence of the four-filter decision, not the starting point. If the regime read flips to −GEX, you would not want this short-gamma profile at all.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The ONE cell where all four filters align for the worked trade: fundamental view = neutral-to-bullish, GEX regime = positive (pin/mean-revert), skew = steep (downside puts rich), IVR = elevated (IV high in its own range — something worth selling) on a liquid chain, AND no earnings/macro catalyst inside the DTE. That is the high-conviction confluence. It FAILS when the filters disagree and you force a trade anyway — e.g. selling downside premium into a −GEX trend-amplifying regime (a small drop becomes a cascade as dealers sell into weakness), selling vega when IVR is low so you are collecting a thin credit for a fat tail of risk, or trying to leg into a thin/wide chain where bid/ask alone destroys the credit. The skill is having the discipline to stand down when filters conflict — most of the edge is in the trades you don't take.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Late-2023 → H1-2024 "+GEX grind" vs the Aug-5-2024 −GEX break (verified context). For most of that stretch SPX sat in an assumed dealer-long-gamma, positive-GEX regime (low realized vol, shallow dips bought) — exactly the tape where the four-filter put-credit-spread thrives: steep skew let you sell rich downside, and pinning kept short gamma safe. Then Aug 5 2024 flipped it: the yen-carry unwind pushed the tape into a −GEX, trend-amplifying state; VIX spiked to an intraday/indicative ~65 (NOT the close — it closed ~38; the ~65 was an early-session indicative print, and carry was a driver, not the sole cause). Anyone mechanically selling downside premium without re-checking the gamma regime got steamrolled as a small drop cascaded. Lesson: the same structure is high-edge in +GEX and account-threatening in −GEX — the regime filter is what makes or breaks the trade, and it can flip in a day.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

ParamDefault
Entry IVR + liquidity gateIVR > 30 (something rich to sell) AND tight bid/ask + deep OI on both legs
Delta / strikeShort leg at ~20–25Δ on the side skew makes richest; long leg $5 further OTM
DTE window30–45 DTE entry (single expiry); manage by 21 DTE
Profit targetClose at 50% of max credit
Stop / time-stopStop at 2× credit loss OR if GEX regime flips to −GEX; time-stop at 21 DTE
SizingDefined-risk: risk ≤1% of account per spread (max loss basis); cut size further in mixed-signal regimes
Defend triggerActionResulting structureNew risk
Spot tests short strike, regime still +GEXRoll the spread down-and-out for a creditNew PCS, lower strikes, later expiryDefers risk; more time short gamma/vega
GEX flips to −GEX (trend regime)Close the spread — don't fight a trend-amplifying tapeFlatRealize a smaller loss vs cascade risk
IVR collapses / skew flattens (credit no longer rich)Close at remaining profit or convert to a fly to cap riskIron fly / flatLocks gains; narrower profit zone
Fully-worked capstone ticket — Underlying SPY @ $100 (normalized). Filters: (1) fundamental = neutral-bullish; (2) GEX = positive (pin regime, est. from a free GEX provider — inference, not measurement); (3) skew = steep (25Δ put IV richer than 25Δ call); (4) IVR = 38 (elevated — real premium to sell) on a liquid chain (penny-wide, deep OI); single expiry, no catalyst in 35 days. Trade: sell 1× 35-DTE $95 put / buy 1× same-expiry $90 put (5-wide). Net credit $0.90 = $90. Max loss $410. BPR ≈ $410 (defined-risk). Breakeven $94.10. Est. POP ~70%. Target: buy back at $0.45 (50%). Stop: close at $1.80 debit (2×) OR immediately if GEX flips −GEX. Roll trigger: spot at $95 with regime still +GEX → roll down-and-out for a credit. Exit: 21 DTE if neither target nor stop hit. Every parameter traces to one of the four filters — that traceability IS the capstone skill.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen for liquid underlyings where you can actually read all four filters (as-of 2026-06-17): options OI > 5,000, bid/ask < 3% of mid, IVR > 30, measurable skew, no earnings within DTE, AND a freely-available GEX/dealer-positioning read. Free tools: SpotGamma/Menthor Q free tiers or barchart for a GEX regime proxy (treat as inference), your broker for skew/IVR. Best-fit candidates — deep, dealer-positioning-covered names (est. as-of 2026-06-17): SPY and QQQ (most-mapped dealer flow), and NVDA (liquid single-name skew/IVR to practice on, but watch earnings). Avoid thin names — you cannot read dealer positioning where there is no positioning to read, and a wide bid/ask kills the credit before you start.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The blow-up is over-trusting one inferred filter — especially GEX. GEX is an inference built on an assumed dealer-long model with stale OI; sign it wrong (providers differ) and you'll sell short gamma into the exact −GEX regime that cascades against you (the Aug-5-2024 trap). Second gotcha: forcing a trade when filters conflict — the framework's value is as a veto, not a green light. Third: execution / liquidity on multi-leg spreads — bid/ask is the largest certain cost, which is why liquidity is built into filter (4) rather than left as an afterthought.

Honest edge — Confirm/deny metric: log every capstone trade with its four-filter state at entry; over ≥50 trades, does requiring all four filters to align beat trading the same structure on the directional thesis alone, after costs? If alignment adds positive expectancy, the synthesis is real; if not, the extra filters are theater. Verdict: Skill-dependent. The fundamental view + disciplined regime/skew/IVR-and-liquidity veto can add genuine edge for a patient trader; mechanically chasing GEX prints is Crowded/Pro-only. The edge is in the discipline of standing down, not in any single inferred number.

04

Capstones — selecting, hedging, and proving your edge

整合
Capstone

Strategy Selector 策略選擇器

A decision tree that maps your three inputs — directional view, IV regime, and event/expected-move — onto the ONE structure that fits, so you stop reaching for a familiar trade in the wrong regime.

Process, not edge
Prereq: Greeks · IV & IVR · Parity · every L1–L4 module this routes into

① 機制 Mechanism

The selector is a pure routing function. You supply three discrete inputs and it returns a structure; it adds no edge of its own — its value is forcing you to name your view and your vol assumption before you pick legs, instead of defaulting to whatever you traded last. The three axes are MECE on purpose: (1) Direction {up / flat / down}; (2) IV regime = IV Rank/Percentile plus the harder judgment of implied vs your forward-realized estimate (high IVR is necessary but not sufficient to be short vol — see F2); (3) Event & expected move — is there a binary catalyst inside your DTE, and do you expect a BIG move (long-gamma territory) or a SMALL move / pin near a strike (short-gamma territory)? The event axis is never just yes/no — it always carries an expected-move-size read, because a calendar and a straddle both "trade an event" yet have opposite gamma signs. The single most common retail error the tree blocks: reaching for a short-gamma premium-collection structure (calendar, short strangle) into a binary where you actually expect a big move.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

No single payoff — the selector's "payoff" is the decision tree itself. The tree's leaves carry the vega-sign discipline from F2: long-premium leaves (θ-negative, long-vol) only when you expect IV or spot to move your way faster than θ bleeds; short-premium leaves (θ-positive, short-vol) only when IVR is elevated and you judge implied > forward realized. One leaf — the calendar — sits in neither family cleanly: it is long-vega but short-gamma and net θ-positive near its strike, a term-structure / IV-crush bet that wants spot to pin at the strike (a small realized move), not a directional or pure long-premium trade. Critically, the calendar branch is NOT one leaf — it forks into THREE mutually-exclusive outcomes: (A) STANDARD calendar (normal upward term structure, no catalyst) → a theta-differential trade; (B) EVENT calendar (front IV rich into a catalyst) → an IV-crush trade; and (C) NO-TRADE / stand-down (big expected move, stale/free-only GEX data, or conflicting signals) → put on no calendar at all. A and B share the same legs but have completely different edge sources; C shares the family but routes you OUT of it because a big move is the calendar's enemy (see facets ③ and ④).

Gated options-strategy selector: each gate (directional view → IV regime → expected move + event) narrows the field before a leaf fires. The calendar family is fully expanded into three distinct outcomes — Calendar A (standard long-vega/short-gamma theta-differential when the IV term structure is normally upward-sloping and there's no catalyst), Calendar B (the event/IV-crush variant when the front month is rich), and the red STAND DOWN / NO-TRADE leaf, since a big move (or only stale/free data, or conflicting signals) is the calendar's enemy. Long gamma, the iron condor, and the debit/credit directional pair round out the tradeable branches.
3input axes (MECE)
12+terminal structures
3calendar leaves (A·B·C)

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Read the tree by the Greek it expresses, not the name. Long premium (Δ-direction + long Γ + long V, short Θ): long call/put, debit verticals, long straddle/strangle, backspreads, 0DTE long options. Short premium (short Γ + short V, long Θ): CC, CSP, Wheel, credit verticals, iron condor/fly, short strangle, ratio spreads, 0DTE credit spreads. The calendar (and its directional cousin the diagonal) is its own family — long V but short Γ and net θ-positive near the strike: it does NOT belong in the long-premium θ-negative bucket; it profits from spot pinning the strike while the long back-month holds its value, and its worst enemy is a large spot move away from the strike, where short gamma drives max loss (a term-structure bet, see L3-5). Because short gamma is fatal on a gap, the calendar family carries a hard third exit: when you expect a big move, when only stale or free-tier GEX data is available to judge pinning, or when the axes conflict, the correct calendar "structure" is no calendar (the STAND-DOWN leaf). The collar/PMCC sit in the middle (net long-delta, financed; both are multi-expiry on the PMCC side; the PMCC is itself a deep-ITM diagonal). The decisive split for the two main families is vega sign + gamma sign: are you being paid Θ to be short realized-vol risk, or paying Θ to own convexity?

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

The selector "works" when you input an honest IV-vs-forward-realized read AND an honest expected-move-size read; it "fails" silently when you let IVR alone decide or treat an event as direction-only. Worked routes: Up + low IVR + no event → debit call vertical or PMCC (own cheap convexity / leverage). Flat + high IVR + no event → iron condor / short strangle (harvest VRP). Down + low IVR + own the stock → collar or protective put (cheap insurance). Event in DTE + you expect a BIG move → long straddle/strangle/backspread (you want long gamma into the gap — a calendar's short gamma is killed by a large move). Up + you already own 100sh + high IVR → covered call — but route with the dominant-risk warning: a covered call is long-delta first (the long stock, ~+0.7..+1.0 net delta), capped on top, only premium-cushioned on the downside; it is wrong in a big rally (capped) AND offers little protection in a big selloff. The trap cell: flat + LOW IVR — no premium to sell and no catalyst to buy; the correct answer is often "no trade."

The calendar, stated canonically — THREE distinct outcomes. A long calendar always SELLS the near-dated option and BUYS the far-dated option at the same strike; it is always long-vega, short-gamma, positive-theta near the strike, with max loss on a large move away from the strike. The calendar branch resolves to exactly three leaves — A (Standard), B (Event), and C (No-trade / stand-down) — and they must never be conflated:

Calendar leafRegime / term structureRoute & where the edge is
A — STANDARD calendar (skill-dependent, no event)Normal upward SPX-options IV term structure: near IV < far IV (the usual state); neutral / pin; small expected move; no catalyst in DTEPut it on (long-vega / short-gamma theta-differential trade). Edge = the theta differential: the cheaper-IV near you SOLD decays faster than the richer-vega far you BOUGHT. No vol-term edge — you sold the CHEAPER near, not a rich one — so you rely on the underlying pinning near the strike and on back-month IV not falling.
B — EVENT calendar (front-rich into a catalyst)Flat or backwardated front: near IV ≥ far IV (e.g. into earnings); a binary catalyst inside DTE; small expected post-event movePut it on (sell the rich front for the IV crush). You SELL the elevated / rich near IV and BUY the cheaper far; you profit from the post-event IV crush of the front while the far holds — provided the underlying stays near the strike. "Sell the rich near" applies only here.
C — NO-TRADE / STAND-DOWNBig expected move; OR only stale / free-only GEX data to judge the pin; OR conflicting signals across the axesDo NOT put on a calendar. A big move is the calendar's enemy — short gamma is overwhelmed regardless of how rich the front was, and you cannot trust a pin you can't measure. Route to a long-gamma structure (if big move) or sit out (if data/signals are illegible).

Three traps the tree blocks: (1) never call leaf A "sell the rich near" — on the normal upward curve the calendar sells the cheaper near for faster theta; (2) never route a calendar (A or B) into an event where you expect a big move — short gamma loses on the gap regardless of how rich the front was; (3) never force leaf A or B when you only have stale/free GEX or conflicting axes — that is leaf C, stand down.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Regime mismatch, generic but dated: heading into a mega-cap earnings print (e.g., NVDA reporting late Feb 2025, IV elevated into the event — illustrative/reconstructed est.), a trader who likes selling premium puts on a short strangle the day before. The selector routes event = yes with an honest expected-move read away from naked short-premium and toward a defined-risk or post-event structure, because the short strangle is short gamma into a gap and short vega into a guaranteed IV crush that can still be swamped by the move. The verified, repeatable fact is the pattern, not the P&L: across single names, front-month IV systematically ramps into earnings and crushes after (see L3-7); the selector exists to stop you from being short gamma at the worst moment — and equally to stop you from routing a short-gamma calendar into a print where you expect a big move (that is leaf C, stand down). Lesson: the structure must match the event and expected-move axis, not just your price view.

Sources: CBOE / Market Chameleon earnings-IV behavior (front-month IV ramp-and-crush pattern); Nasdaq company calendar (NVDA fiscal-Q4 report date, late Feb 2025).

Worked decision ticket — Inputs: view = mildly bullish over 30–45d on a liquid name; IVR = 68 (rich); event = none in window, no big move expected. Tree route: up + high IVR + no event → bull put credit spread (sell premium with a directional tilt, defined risk). Hands off to L2-2 for strikes/sizing: e.g., 30Δ short put / 15Δ long put, 35 DTE, target 50% of max credit, stop at 2× credit, time-stop 21 DTE. The selector's job ends once the regime → structure mapping is locked.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

The selector screens regimes, not tickers — but it gates which screen you run next. Concrete sequence on a FREE stack: pull IVR from Market Chameleon (free tier) or barchart.com IV-Rank screener → bucket the name (cheap/mid/rich) → check the earnings calendar (earningswhispers.com) for an event inside your DTE and read the implied expected move → only then open the chosen module's liquidity screen (OI>1,000, bid/ask<10% of mid). The event + term-structure check is what splits the THREE calendar leaves: an event in DTE with a SMALL expected move and a rich front routes to the EVENT calendar (leaf B); NO event on a normal upward term structure with a small move routes to the STANDARD calendar (leaf A); but a BIG expected move, or only stale/free-tier GEX to judge the pin, or conflicting reads routes to NO-TRADE / stand-down (leaf C) — you do not put on a calendar. As-of 2026-06-17 (est.), broad-index IV sits low (VIX ~16), so the tree is currently routing away from index short-premium and toward single-name catalysts and cheap directional convexity.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: a decision tree feels like edge but is only discipline. Its single failure mode is a dishonest input — overstating your directional conviction, calling IV "rich" off IVR alone when the high IVR is correctly pricing a real forward-realized move (then you sell vol that was cheap, not rich), or treating an event as a price bet while ignoring expected-move size (then you route short gamma into a big move). The calendar fork is where this bites hardest: routing a STANDARD (leaf A) calendar as if it had the EVENT (leaf B) "sell the rich near" edge, or routing either A or B into an event where you expect a big move (or off stale/free GEX) when the honest answer is leaf C — NO-TRADE / stand-down. It cannot tell you your view is wrong; it can only stop you from expressing a right view in the wrong vega or gamma sign — and when the inputs conflict or a big move looms, it stands you down rather than forcing a trade.

Honest edge — metric: does using the tree reduce your rate of regime-mismatched trades (short-gamma into big-move events, long-premium into rich-IV-no-catalyst, STANDARD calendar mislabeled as EVENT, calendar forced when leaf C says stand down) versus your logged baseline? Verdict: Process discipline, not edge — it removes self-inflicted negative-EV trades; it does not create positive EV.

Capstone

Portfolio Hedging 投資組合避險

Manage the book's net market exposure — beta-weighted delta and net vega — as ONE number, then cut tail risk with collars and index overlays instead of guessing position by position.

Skill-dependent
Prereq: Position Greeks · Risk & sizing · Protective put / collar

① 機制 Mechanism

You stop thinking in individual tickers and collapse the whole book into two scalars. Beta-weighted delta converts every position's delta into benchmark-equivalent shares (default benchmark = SPY) so you can read your true directional market exposure as a single number: a beta-weighted delta of +200 against SPY means the book behaves roughly like long 200 SPY shares for a 1-point SPY move. Hedge that net number at the portfolio level (short SPY/ES, long index puts, or a put spread) rather than delta-neutralizing every name. The second scalar is net vega — your aggregate long/short volatility exposure across all options; a book can look delta-flat yet be dangerously short vega (a hidden short-vol bet that detonates on a vol spike). The math caveat (graded): beta-weighting assumes a stable single-factor beta to SPY; in a real crash correlations spike toward 1 and betas dislocate, so the hedge that looked perfect at β=1.1 under-hedges exactly when you need it. Treat the number as a steering wheel, not a guarantee.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

Shown per-share on a normalized underlying as the canonical concentrated-position hedge — the zero-cost collar: long stock + long protective put (the floor) + short OTM call (finances the put). Payoff is the classic collared brick: downside capped at the put strike, upside capped at the call strike, the middle is just stock.

The put-spread collar (long put + short lower put + short call) gives back some left-tail protection below the lower put strike in exchange for a wider upside cap or a net credit — protection that fails exactly in the crash you bought it for. See the engine render the re-opened deep-left tail:

95collar floor (put strike)
108collar cap (call strike)
<85put-spread tail re-opens

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

Book-level, not leg-level. A long-equity book is net long beta-weighted delta and typically net short vega (equity vol rises as price falls — your longs lose money precisely as the vol you'd want to own gets expensive). Adding long index puts pushes delta down, vega up, gamma up, theta down — you pay carry for crash convexity. A collar is net long delta but bounded (gamma turns negative near the short call, positive near the long put). The discipline: set hard limits on both scalars — e.g., keep beta-weighted delta inside a band and cap net short vega — and rebalance when either breaches.

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

Works: when you have a diversified or concentrated long book and want to dial systematic market risk down cheaply without selling stock (tax, conviction, or low-basis reasons). Index/ES overlays are the cheapest, most-liquid way to shed net beta-weighted delta; collars are best for a single concentrated low-basis position you can't or won't sell. Fails: (1) on a concentrated single-stock book hedged with an index put — you're left with the idiosyncratic risk (the stock craters on its own news while the index is flat = basis risk, the hedge pays nothing); (2) when correlations spike and your beta estimate breaks; (3) chronic over-hedging — a permanent short-delta/long-put overlay bleeds carry and quietly caps your equity compounding (the cost of insurance you rarely collect on).

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

Use the broad-index vol context as the dated anchor (as-of 2026-06-17): VIX ~16 (verified ~16.4, Jun 2026, GuruFocus), near its long-run median of ~17.6 and well below crisis prints (record 82.69). At 16-vol, index put protection is historically cheap — the regime where systematic hedging via collars/put-spreads is most attractive (you're buying insurance when it's not in demand). The contrast case: hedging a concentrated single-name book (say a large low-basis position in one mega-cap) with SPY puts would have left you fully exposed to that name's own gap-downs while paying premium for index protection that didn't fire — the textbook basis-risk failure. Lesson: match the hedge instrument to the risk you actually carry (idiosyncratic → single-name; systematic → index).

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Worked hedge ticket — Book: net beta-weighted delta = +450 SPY-deltas (≈ long 450 SPY shares of market risk), SPY at 100 (normalized). Goal: halve systematic exposure into an uncertain macro window without selling the underlying longs. Trade: buy 2× SPY 95-strike puts (60 DTE) ≈ −60 delta, AND sell ES/short 200 SPY-equivalent to cut net BW-delta to ~+190. Net result: directional risk roughly halved, a long-put tail overlay added (vega +, carry cost paid). Exit: lift the overlay when the macro window passes or BW-delta drifts back inside band; roll puts at 21 DTE if still wanted.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Hedge-instrument screen: for systematic overlays use the most liquid index vehicle (SPY/SPX/ES — penny-wide, OI in the millions). For a concentrated name, require the single-name option chain to clear OI>2,000 and bid/ask<8% of mid at your target strikes so the hedge is cheap to put on AND take off. FREE tools: tastytrade / thinkorswim show beta-weighted delta natively (set the beta-weight symbol to SPY); portfoliovisualizer for book beta. As-of 2026-06-17 (est.), SPX/SPY puts are cheap (VIX ~16) → favorable window to establish index tail overlays; single-name collars best on low-basis concentrated holdings you won't sell.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha (graded): beta-weighted delta and the put-spread collar both fail in the same scenario — a real crash. Betas dislocate and correlations rush to 1 (your index hedge under-covers), and the put-spread collar's short lower put re-opens the deep-left tail right where you needed protection. You can hedge yourself into a false sense of safety. Secondary killer: chronic carry bleed from over-hedging silently erodes the compounding the longs were there to deliver.

Honest edge — metric: over a full cycle, does your hedged book's drawdown-reduction per dollar of hedge carry paid beat simply holding less stock? If carry bleed > drawdowns avoided, the hedge is a tax. Verdict: Skill-dependent — beta-weighting is genuinely useful risk measurement; the hedging edge is real only with disciplined sizing, instrument-to-risk matching, and tactical (not permanent) overlays.

Capstone

Verify-Your-Edge Loop 驗證你的優勢

A trade journal turned into a falsification engine: log the right fields, clear a two-tier sample bar (enough to REVIEW a strategy, far more to TRUST its edge), compare P50 to outcome, and only graduate paper → real once the data — not the feeling — says you have edge.

Genuine (the meta-edge)
Prereq: POP vs EV / P50 / negative skew · Slippage · every module's honest-edge tag

① 機制 Mechanism

This is the engine that turns "learn by trading each concept" into actual learning instead of expensive entertainment. Mechanism: for every trade you log a fixed field list at entry (so it can't be rationalized later) and at exit, then run a few simple comparisons that try to falsify your edge rather than confirm it. The core comparison is P50 (your modeled median / expected outcome) vs the realized outcome, aggregated across enough trades that variance doesn't fool you. Crucially there are two sample bars, not one: a smaller sample is enough to REVIEW a strategy (sanity-check your process, spot discipline leaks, see gross slippage) — roughly ≈30 symmetric trades or ≈50+ for short-premium — but a much larger sample is needed to TRUST the edge estimate itself — on the order of ≈200. The reason the trust bar is so much higher for options specifically: short-premium P&L is negatively skewed — you win small often and lose big rarely. That structure, combined with ordinary variance, means a small sample of wins will over-state your edge: you can be 9-for-10 and still negative-EV because the 10th trade was the one that mattered (the tail that eats the year, F2). The loop exists to stop you from confusing a not-yet-arrived tail with skill — so it reviews early and trusts late.

② 損益結構 Payoff & breakevens

The "payoff" here is your edge estimate as a distribution, not a single number. What you're plotting over time is realized EV-per-trade with its confidence band shrinking as N grows — and the brutal asymmetry of small samples under negative skew, where the point estimate sits flatteringly high until a tail event drags it to truth. Read the two-tier framing straight off this curve: by the review bar (≈30/≈50+) the band is still wide enough that the strategy is only sanity-checked, not validated; only near the trust bar (≈200) has the band narrowed and survived enough vol regimes that the realized EV is something you can act on. The funnel narrowing with N is the whole point: small samples promise, large samples prove.

Convergence funnel: a strategy's measured edge starts wide and biased high at small N (red whisker, overstated), then the confidence band narrows and the point estimate drifts down toward a modest true value as N grows along a log x-axis. Negative skew plus thin samples flatter your edge — accumulate a large trade count before trusting the number.
≈30 / 50+min N to REVIEW (symmetric / short-prem)
≈200min N to TRUST the edge estimate
P50vs realized = the test

③ 希臘字母特徵 Greeks signature

The loop has no Greeks of its own — it audits the Greeks you claimed. Log the entry Greeks (Δ/Θ/V and IVR) so you can later test whether your short-vol trades actually collected the variance risk premium (VRP) you assumed. VRP is real and persistent (implied vol has historically averaged above subsequent realized vol — the insurance-like, negatively-convex premium documented across equity indices), but it is fundamentally compensation for being short GAMMA (bearing realized-vol / gap risk); the vega leg is a separate exposure that is often hedgeable, and you can be short gamma while near vega-neutral. Because the premium pays you for absorbing rare large realized moves, a few quiet months — or even a full review-sized sample — will flatter you. This is exactly why VRP harvesting needs the higher trust bar (≈200) before you believe the number: the premium only proves out across enough realized-vol regimes for the tail to be represented. Logging entry IV vs realized-vol-over-hold is how you separate "I harvested a genuine premium" from "vol just happened to stay low on my watch."

④ 適用情境 When it works / fails

Works: as the gate on every other module in this dashboard — each one ships an honest-edge metric, and this loop is where you actually measure it on your own fills. It is most valuable precisely on the strategies whose edge is "skill-dependent" (gamma scalping, short-vol/VRP, anything execution-sensitive): only your logged slippage-vs-mid and P50-vs-outcome can tell you whether you specifically capture the edge after your costs. The two-tier sample bar is what makes it honest: at the review sample you may refine process, but you do not yet claim edge; you claim edge only at the trust sample (≈200). Fails: when you log outcomes but not entry assumptions (you can't compare expected to realized); when you treat the review bar as the trust bar and graduate a strategy on ≈30–50 hot trades (negative skew + variance over-state small-N edge); or when you let "I felt good about it" graduate a strategy the data hasn't cleared. The loop only works if entry fields are immutable once written.

⑤ 真實案例 Real-world case

The canonical dated cautionary tale for "small-N over-states short-vol edge" is Volmageddon, Feb 5 2018: short-vol products that had printed steady gains for years detonated in a single session — the XIV ETN's indicative value collapsed roughly ~96% after the close as the VIX-futures complex spiked, and Credit Suisse subsequently announced its acceleration/termination (verify exact indicative-value mechanics and after-hours sequence — many summaries misstate the % and the product mechanics; treat as est.). Whatever the precise figure, the structural lesson is exact: a multi-year track record of small short-vol wins — a sample far past any review bar — was not evidence of edge, because the left tail simply hadn't arrived yet. That is precisely why the loop separates the two bars: enough trades to review a strategy is nowhere near enough to trust it under negative skew, and no win streak — at any N short of a full volatility cycle — graduates a strategy. (As-of context 2026-06-17: VIX ~16, a quiet regime — exactly when short-vol logs look deceptively good.)

Sources: Credit Suisse XIV ETN acceleration announcement (Feb 6 2018); SEC/Cboe post-event materials on the Feb 5 2018 VIX-futures spike.

⑥ 進場／管理／出場 Playbook

Journal field list (the deliverable) — At entry: date/time · ticker · structure · directional view · entry IVR · entry IV (per leg) · entry Δ/Θ/V (position) · DTE · contracts/size · net credit/debit · BPR/max loss · breakeven(s) · modeled P50 & POP & EV · pre-set target / stop / roll trigger / time-stop · why this regime (link to C1 route). At exit: exit date · exit price/credit · realized P&L · realized vol over the hold · slippage vs mid (entry & exit) · did I follow the plan? (Y/N) · P50-vs-outcome delta · one-line lesson. Aggregate (every 10 trades): running N vs the REVIEW bar (≈30/50) and the TRUST bar (≈200) · realized EV/trade · win-rate · avg win / avg loss · worst loss vs modeled max · cumulative slippage drag · discipline-breach count. This field list is the verify-edge engine — fill it for every concept you trade and the dashboard's honest-edge tags become your measured numbers.

⑦ 選標的 Fit-target screen

Screen your own log, not the market. Tooling: a plain spreadsheet (Google Sheets) with the field list above is sufficient; tradervue or edgewonk (paid) automate the aggregates; tastytrade exports fills with entry Greeks. Minimum viable: one row per trade, frozen entry block, computed P50-vs-outcome and slippage columns, plus a running-N readout against both bars. Gate to graduate a concept from paper to real money: ≥20 logged paper trades in that exact structure, 0 discipline breaches, and realized EV non-negative — then start at 1/3 size. Keep that 1/3 size until you clear the REVIEW bar (≈30/50+) live; only AFFIRM the edge and move toward full size once you clear the TRUST bar (≈200) live with realized EV still positive after slippage.

⑧ 風險與卡點 Risks & the gotcha

The gotcha: the negative-skew sample trap — collapsing the two bars into one. With short-premium strategies you will hit the review bar feeling like a genius because the tail hasn't shown up, and the temptation to treat that review-sized sample as a trust-sized verdict and upsize before a full cycle of volatility regimes (the ≈200 bar) is exactly how Volmageddon-style blowups happen to disciplined-looking traders. The second gotcha is survivorship in your own log: quietly not-logging the trade you're embarrassed by destroys the whole instrument. The loop only works if every trade is logged at entry, immutably, including the ones that go wrong.

Honest edge — metric: across the TRUST sample (≈200, not the review-sized ≈30/50), is your realized EV/trade positive after logged slippage, and is your worst realized loss within your modeled max? If yes for a strategy, that strategy has edge for you; if no, retire it. Verdict: Genuine — this is the meta-edge. The loop produces no P&L itself, but it is the only thing that converts the rest of the dashboard from knowledge into measured, durable advantage.

Variance, Correlation & Dispersion 變異數・相關性・離散交易
L4-5		Short index vol vs long single-name vol = short correlationGate on implied correlation (Cboe COR3M) top ~25% percentile, not raw IVRDelta-neutral straddles, index-vega-neutral basket; 30-60 DTE per legTake profit on COR3M down ~5-10 pts; hard stop on VIX +10 intraday — cut short-index leg firstPro-onlyCboe COR1M/COR3M (free); Barchart & market-chameleon vol surfacesPAPER/observe only — build implied-vs-realized correlation worksheet; needs PM + variance access
Box / Jelly Roll / Conversion-Reversal 盒式價差・果凍卷・轉換套利
L4-6		Direction-free — a rates/financing trade (synthetic borrow or lend), not a market viewIrrelevant (vega-neutral); gate on implied box yield vs your funding/margin rate insteadΔ≈Γ≈V≈Θ≈0, only rho live; match DTE to financing horizon 30-365 DTE; European/SPX onlyTake when implied yield beats margin rate >0.5% ann.; NO safe stop on American legs — don't trade them as boxesPro-onlyCboe SPX/XSP chains (free) to compute implied box yield; broker margin ratePAPER/observe only — financing trade, no edge; European cash-settled SPX only
Capstone — Dealer-Positioning-Aware Playbook 整合戰術
L4-7		Your fundamental view, vetted by a 4-filter stack (single-expiry: NO term structure)IVR>30 + favorable skew + supportive GEX regime + adequate liquidityShort leg ~20-25Δ on the side skew favors, long leg $5 further OTM; 30-45 DTE entry, manage by 21 DTEClose at 50% max credit; stop at 2x credit OR immediately if GEX flips to -GEX; time-stop 21 DTESkill-dependent — fundamental view filtered by GEX regime + skew + IVR/liquidity (single-expiry: NO term structure)SpotGamma/Menthor Q free tier or Barchart (GEX proxy, inference); Cboe term structure; broker skew/IVRSell a 35-DTE SPY put credit spread only when view + GEX regime + skew + IVR/liquidity all align

Disclaimer & sources

免責與來源
Not investment advice. This is a personal educational artifact for a retail trader's own study and practice. Options involve substantial risk, including assignment, gap, and total-loss risk; undefined-risk structures can lose more than the premium received. Real-world cases are for instruction; numbers are verified against ≥2 public sources where load-bearing and stamped as-of 2026-06-17, with estimates marked (est.). Dealer-positioning / GEX figures are inferences, not measurements. Verify everything against your broker's live data before risking capital. IBKR access is read-only; no trade is ever placed by this tool.
